## Introduction

Another Final Fantasy XII guide? What on earth for?? Why can't I just use my old Prima Guide? Well, that's what I wondered.

Apparently, the Zodiac Age version is vastly different from the original. Not in story, mind you, but in how the game is played. You probably are already aware of that as you now have jobs the characters can select. Specifically, there are twelve jobs, related to the signs of the zodiac, of which each character can have two. But that's not all.

Remember in the original how you had to be so careful not to touch 4 treasure chests or the Zodiac Spear wouldn't appear? Good news: not an issue anymore. Bad news: the Zodiac Spear isn't in the Necrohol of Nabudis anymore. Good news: there's more than one way to get the spear. You see, the whole treasure system is revamped. There are new gear, new gear stats, new treasures, new means of obtaining everything, etc. If you're looking for a specific piece of loot, those old treasure map guides might not point you in the right direction. There are, however, numerous treasure guides for the IZJS version of the game that are very similar to the Zodiac Age maps - so this walkthrough isn't going to be that (but I'll still point you to important loot as it comes up).

This is also not going to be an in-depth guide to the job system. Those already exist too. But if this guide isn't going to be an in-depth difference guide walkthrough, then why bother? Well, let's consider my ....

### Motivation

As with all guides, one of the most important questions to ask is: What does this guide contribute that isn't found elsewhere? That's an even more important question when you look at the number of walkthrough guides that already exist for Final Fantasy XII. The simple answer is none of the guides were easy enough to use for my taste. I got spoiled by the guides created by bover\_87 for several of the other Final Fantasy games, so when I finally picked up a copy of Zodiac Age for PS4 and couldn't find something comparable I was a bit disappointed. I wanted a walkthrough that was HTML for the easy navigation, was exhaustive enough to do whatever I wanted, and didn't make me read through a bunch of optional material in the middle of the main walkthrough (but pointed me in the right direction if I wanted to). So that's what I hope to do. I'll be relying on various other walkthroughs (written and video) along with my own experience (I completed every available side mission on the PS2 version back when the game was first released in the US).

### What This Guide Is

This guide is written specifically for the US release of the PS4 version Final Fantasy XII: The Zodiac Age. I will be providing a play-by-play walkthrough with recommendations on gambits, gear, skills, appropriate timing for sidequests, etc. for the main game. It will include guides on how to accomplish all of the sidequests - especially the major ones such as obtaining the Espers, the Hunt Club, and Marks. It will also include tips for the trickier portions of Trial Mode so that you can get the two trophies associated with that as well. Following this guide should result in receiving a platinum trophy. Following this guide should also help you get a platinum trophy as I will recommend when each task needs to be completed and point out when I earned each trophy.

Anything I deem as a spoiler will be covered using the spoiler cover that you can see through by highlighting or selecting the covered text. Be aware, however, that this game has a lot of intrigue. As such, things that become common knowledge later in the game and would not be covered would very much be a spoiler early in the game. Read ahead at your own risk!

Another thing to note is that GameFaqs guides don't display spoiler covers correctly on phones and tablets. There's nothing I can do about it.

Notification boxes will be used for boss strategies. Boss strategies will include screenshot, boss stats, recommended gambit and gear setups. The steal list will contain two percentages, indicating the probability of stealing that item without and with the Thief’s Cuffs accessory equipped.

I must confess that the guides by bover\_87 are my inspiration, and I hope that I can create something that approaches that quality. I expect that this guide will take some time to complete and, once published, will benefit from feedback. Feel free to send any recommendations my way; but before you do, make sure you know...

### What This Guide Is Not

This guide will not be a plot summary. As much as possible, I will not reveal any story elements. You want to play the game for yourself, not read my experience, right? I will tell you how to get from point A to point B and what you might want to do to be prepared for point B. I will not tell you the content of the dialogue or even the identities of new people you meet until after you've met them. I also won't be explaining the plot. This game is full of intrigue, so enjoy it for yourself!

This guide is not an item list or a treasure percentage or a map guide. That would be way too much time and would not fulfill the goal of this guide: provide an easy-to-follow walkthrough that will allow you to do everything you want without being encumbered by what you don't. I’ll still point out where some treasures might be, particularly if they have a unique, valuable, or important item. I’ll skip several of the ones that have basic healing items.

Similarly, I do not as of right now plan on incorporating a detailed walkthrough of Trial Mode, although I may make some recommendations as to when it may be appropriate to do certain tasks in trial mode. As such, I can almost guarantee that there are some game-breaking power-up guides that take advantage of Trial Mode that would make it redundant if I tried to incorporate all that information.

Also, I will not be recommending strategies that require the abuse of the RNG, although I might point out treasures that you could get if you did. You will not be required to use RNG tricks to use this guide - especially since some people feel like that is cheating. I personally don't care (if it's in the game and you can do it, how is it cheating?), but I know that it can take the fun out of it and some people may just not be able to get it to work properly for some reason or another. In other words, you should be able to play the game however you want and still use this guide.

This guide will not be a collection of every single statistic in the game. This walkthrough is intended for the casual player who wants to get the most out of the game, not the meta-players who can calculate exactly how many seconds it will take to beat Yiazmat with a specific level and set of gear/gambits. If you want to know that much about the game, you probably know far more than I do and don't need the kind of walkthrough that I am trying to create. I appreciate that kind of player, because they figure out all the tricks for someone like me to write a good guide - but you would be a source for (not a consumer of) a guide like this.

Finally, this guide is not for sale. I'm making this for my own pleasure, using the guide editor on gamefaqs. If you see this guide on any other site, please notify me. If I have given permission, it would already have been updated here.

I'm sure there's a ton of other stuff this guide is not (for example, it's not leftovers from last night or an architectural diagram of the Roman Colosseum). This is just a list of the main things that I see included in other walkthroughs that I can say up front won't be found here. I hope that it is as useful to you as the bover\_87 guides have been to me!

### Credits

I did a lot of research while writing this guide. Nothing in here is copied and pasted from any other source, because I typically used multiple sources and my own experience and never accepted anything from a single source. Furthermore, most sources were outdated or didn't directly apply to the Zodiac Age version of the game, so any knowledge gleaned had to be updated. That said, I still want to give credit to all of my sources since many can still be very helpful to you all.

#### TIER 1 sources - very helpful, heavily utilized:

- Final Fantasy Wiki - https://finalfantasy.fandom.com - This was very helpful for getting creature data and validating some treasure lists.
- VG Maps - https://vgmaps.com/Atlas/PS2/index.htm#FinalFantasyXIIInternationalZodiacJobSystem - This was mostly used for help with navigation and treasure locations. It was created for the IZJS version of the game and not the Zodiac Age, so it doesn't match perfectly. It's probably a 99.9% match, so I tried to use my own playthrough to identify any discrepancies.
- Playstation Profiles - https://psnprofiles.com/guide/6250-final-fantasy-xii-the-zodiac-age-trophy-guide - This was mostly used to verify some of the more finicky collectable trophies. The forums here were also helpful.
- bover\_87 - https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/community/bover\_87/contributions/faqs - Okay, so I didn't use him for any guide info, but I definitely am copying his guide format from other Final Fantasy guides. His are terrific, and I wanted a guide for Zodiac Age that looked like he made it - thus this guide.

#### Tier 2 sources - helpful, worth looking into:

- Fuzzfinger Gaming's YouTube full walkthrough - https://www.youtube.com/playlist?list=PLawwJPcwj4dce4qNAwkH0kW6rxWJe30RL - This walkthrough was done before some of the more recent updates that allow you to switch jobs, but is still useful. If something I'm saying isn't clear, you may check out his videos. I would typically only use them as reference if I was about to try something I wasn't very familiar with or forgot to create a save point and was going to have to play a bunch to do further playtesting. My strategy for Trial Mode Stage 100 is related to his, except he used the RNG trick to get a bunch of items that I didn't have for my playthrough. I was committed to providing a guide that would not require it so that anybody could use this guide.
- Brady Games Final Fantasy XII Guide by Barba, Cassady, Epstein and Ehrlichman - published 2006 for PS2, North America version - This was the guide I used back when the game first came out. Obviously, it is dated as the job system is not included and the treasure maps have no resemblance to the Zodiac Age version. Enemy stats, boss strategies, quest rewards, gear, and more are no longer valid. Even so, it was useful to help jog my memory about storyline and when different sidequests would open up. Even those don't match Zodiac Age perfectly, but it still kept me alert.

#### Tier 3 sources - only occasionally consulted, still great content:

- GameFaqs

  - https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/boards/191202-final-fantasy-xii-the-zodiac-age - I never had to post any questions because most of them were already covered somewhere in the forums. Not sure about something? This is a great community to ask.
  - https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ps4/191202-final-fantasy-xii-the-zodiac-age/faqs/76676/trial-mode-stages-1-100 - Auron402 has a Power-Up Guide on GameFaqs that covers the 100 stages of Trial Mode with a focus on the gear you should grab. It has some strategy on how to beat the difficult stages as well. If I got stuck, this is one of the places I'd use for inspiration, although I usually made my own strategy that fit my play style.
- Jegged - There is actually a very big source that I may have under-utilized. I really only looked at a couple of things here, but feel free to check out https://jegged.com/Games/Final-Fantasy-XII/ for a lot of good information with nice screenshots.

  - https://jegged.com/Games/Final-Fantasy-XII/Trophies/ - I didn't use this much, only when I had conflicting information and needed another source to weigh in.
  - https://jegged.com/Games/Final-Fantasy-XII/Trial-Mode/ - This was another Trial Mode source. It wasn't much help for strategies, but it has a comprehensive list of what you can grab at each level.
- GameSkinny - https://www.gameskinny.com/1o5tb/ffxii-the-zodiac-age-hunt-club-and-trophy-rare-game-guide - This was the most user-friendly guide to the Hunt Club that I found.

## Walkthrough Part 1 - The Occupied City

To prepare you for how to read this guide, here are a couple of notes on the layout. When reasonable, the subsections will be named for the different areas (such as in [Nalbina Fortress](#nalbina-fortress)). As much of this game requires going back and forth between areas, at those points the subsections will be named for some story arc that is taking place (such as in [Getting The Help of Old Dalan](#getting-the-help-of-old-dalan)). Sometimes it might be a little of both (such as in [Your First Hunt](#walkthrough-part-1---the-occupied-city)). Lists of treasures, loot, and enemies will appear for each area or story arc as appropriate. There will not be comprehensive lists of treasures simply because not all treasure boxes are guaranteed to spawn and no two playthroughs will have the same experience. When there is a specific valuable / unique item you can gain or a story-related item, those will be noted as "notable". Now that you know what to expect, start your new game and enjoy the introductory scenes!

### Nalbina Fortress

#### Aerial Gardens

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |

Once you have control of your character after the opening videos, you will begin to be introduced to the game controls. Start by rotating your R3 stick when instructed, and then moving using L3 when instructed. Talk with the NPC to your north to learn about talk icons, then with the NPC further east. Interact with the gate just to the north and proceed into the next area.

#### Inner Ward

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |
| **Enemies** | Imperial Swordsman, Air Cutter Remora |

Upon entry, you will be placed into your first battle. You'll be given basic instructions on how to read the lines connecting the different battle participants, as well as how to carry out basic actions in battle. Press X to bring up your action menu and select X and choose to attack incoming Imperial Swordsman. Don't worry about your health at this point as one of the other soldiers will throw potions at you whenever your health begins to drop. Afterward, proceed across the bridge for a short scene and your first boss battle. You can identify a boss by the long red health bar at the top of the screen.

> ##### Boss: Air Cutter Remora
>
>
> | **Level** | 10 | **LP** | 0 | **CP** | 0 |
> | **HP** | 2200 | **Steal** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Oil, Immobilize, Libra |
>
> This battle is really just to help you recognize a boss battle. You can use Thunder magic if you want to finish the battle quickly, but you don't even need to heal. It will periodically use a multi-target (MT) attack called Remora Strike, but as long as the other soldiers are with you they will keep you healed with potions. The fight ends shortly after half the health has been removed when the captain uses a special attack that you will learn more about later in the game.

After your captain finishes off the boss battle, there will be a brief scene and then three more Imperial Swordsmen to fight. Approach the exit for a tutorial on the map feature. The Zodiac Age version introduces a new option to overlay the entire area map over your play screen and not just the local area in the top left. To toggle this on, press L3. To view the map as previously in the original version, press the touch screen on your controller. Proceed into the next area for another scene.

#### Lower Apartments

| **Treasures** | 2 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |
| **Enemies** | Imperial Swordsman, Imperial Magus |

Enemies in this area will respawn, so you can fight endlessly - but there is no value in it. There are two treasure chests that will appear with potions that you can grab. One is in a room on the east wall of the entry corridor just past the first intersection where you begin. After grabbing that potion, turn left at the next intersection and then turn back north. If you continue north past the stairwell, you can find the second potion in the north alcove on the back hallway. Once you've grabbed both, use the stair to reach the next area.

#### Upper Apartments

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |

Upon entry into the **Upper Apartments**, you'll be introduced to the Save Crystal (the giant blue gem). There is a hallway to the east of the stairwell, but it's empty. Move toward the stairs progressing up for another scene.

When you're ready to proceed,

#### The Highhall

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |
| **Enemies** | Imperial Swordsman |

If you didn't return to the **Lower Apartments** previously, this is your first solo fight. You have to take out 3 Imperial Swordsmen, but as long as you select to attack one as soon as finishing off another you shouldn't need to heal.

If you plan on using the RNG manipulation tricks to get some rare, extremely powerful items early in the game, make sure you return to the save crystal and save your game in a couple of slots now that you have Reks alone. This is your "Reks" save. If you didn't already pick up the 2 potions in the Lower Apartments, you may still return, but you will be fighting solo and will be responsible for your own healing now.

More scenes to follow.

### Red & Rotten in the Desert

#### Garamsythe Waterway: Overflow Cloaca

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | none |
| **Enemies** | Dire Rat |

Once you regain control, you will need to kill the three dire rats. They tend to attack one at a time, so be sure to target the rat that is targeting you. Go ahead and steal from them. This gives you some easy gil and helps you make progress on the Master Thief trophy. Once you kill all three, another scene will begin automatically.

#### Rabanastre: East End

| **Treasures** | 0 |
| **Notable Loot** | Clan Primer, Orrachea Armlet, Writ of Transit, License Board |

After the scenes, you will be told to go to Migelo's place. You might as well head straight there as all other exits are blocked. Available exits are shown with green lines, while blocked exits are shown with red lines. Migelo is in the southeastern-most portion of East End and a scene will begin as soon as you approach him. After the scene, many of the exits become available, but there isn't really anything that can be done and you still can't leave the city.

Instead, head north until you find the Sandsea in the northeastern-most portion of **East End** and head inside for a scene. Follow the dialogue to learn about the Notice Board and hunts. The hunt you are given is the only mandatory hunt in the game and does not require you to track down the poster of the bill.

You'll be given a handful of key items in the process (Clan Primer, Writ of Transit, and License Board access), as well as your first accessory (Orrachea Armlet). You can't equip it, however, until you unlock it on a license board - which can only happen once you've chosen a job.

There isn't much information given to you at this point about choosing a job other than you can't change your mind - which is only true for now. You can check out some other guides on choosing jobs (for example, https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/boards/191202-final-fantasy-xii-the-zodiac-age/75477310 gives a brief but great analysis) or watch some videos that break them down - or I can just make recommendations for you. If you want something well-suited to Vaan that should keep you pretty strong, choose Knight. Not only that, if you use Trial Mode, you can get the very strong sword Kakarta early on, which only Knights can equip (but take a lot of license points to unlock). If you want something more fitted to his character, choose Shikari. Everything is fine and soon enough you'll be able to play around with it all.

After you leave the Sandsea, all of the shops should be open, but you probably don't have the gil to do much shopping yet. Instead, head back south and exit into...

#### Southern Plaza

| Notable Loot | Small Package |

Before you just pass through, talk to the Bangaa (long ears, long snout) in conversation with an Imperial Guard by the fountain and accept his request. He'll give you a Small Package. Now head to the east toward the gate to trigger a scene. Afterward, you'll find yourself in the...

#### Eastgate

There is a save crystal to use before setting out into the **Dalmasca Estersand**. If you haven't already, you can try your hand at Trial Mode here. There are lots of goodies to get, if you're strong enough. You can also get a lot of funding pretty quickly by getting Diamond Armlets from the chest in level 1 and selling them if you don't want to farm in the **Estersand**. It's really up to you how you plan on making your gil. You can also deliver that small package to the Hapless Merchant having a discussion with an Imperial Guardsman. Enjoy your reward of nothing whatsoever. Continue eastward into the ...

#### Dalmasca Estersand: The Stepping

| **Treasures** | 6 |
| **Notable Loot** | handful of Galbana Lilies |
| **Enemies** | Cactite, Wolf, Cockatrice, Wild Saurian, Rogue Tomato \[hunt\] |

Your main objective is to find and eliminate the Rogue Tomato - neither of which are difficult tasks. Before we get going, however, a couple of things to note. Cactuars and the lone Wild Saurian are not aggressive to you unless you attack them. Cactuars are nice easy prey to get some loot and experience...Wild Saurians are not. The Wild Saurians may be observed fighting a Wolf periodically. Another note is that the treasure chests are not guaranteed to appear. If they do, you may be able to begin stocking up on some potions, antidotes, and phoenix downs. This is useful if you aren't planning on getting filthy rich from Trial Mode.

Before heading straight to the Rogue Tomato, it's probably a good idea to be at least level 2 or 3. It doesn't take much time and it would give you a chance to explore the area and get comfortable with the commands anyway. You can attempt stealing from most of the foes in the area too (as long as you leave the Wild Saurian alone) for loot that you can sell. You may even get lucky and get a Pointy Hat from a Wolf, a Helm that requires the Mystic Armor 2 license. If you plan on assigning jobs early, this is a nice freebie, but I'd save it for your second party member. If your HP starts to drop, head back to the Save Crystal at the **Eastgate.**

Once you are at level 2 or 3, save your game and come back into **The Stepping**. You may have already seen the Rogue Tomato running around on an outcrop in the middle of the area. Fight off the Wolves around the outcrop and then approach the Rogue Tomato to initiate the encounter.

> ##### Hunt: Rogue Tomato
>
>
> | **Level** | 2 | **LP** | 3 | **CP** | 180 |
> | **HP** | 134 | **Steal** | Pebble (55/80%), Fire Stone (10/30%), Potion (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Water |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Sleep, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disease, Disable, Immobilize, Sap, Slow, Berserk, Libra |
>
> If you're level 3, this should be a breeze. Other than regular attacks, it may use the skill Ram, which will do about 10 damage to Vaan if he hasn't upgraded any gear yet. It will also attempt to use the skill Flame Breath once when its HP drops below 40%. This can do around 25 damage. Before you start attacking, go ahead and steal. After you get an item, whack at it until it's health is half full and it will jump off the outcrop. As long as you cleared the wolves, you can run down and finish it off. Otherwise you may have to fight your way down there. If you take too long, the Tomato may have regenerated most or all of its health. You can steal a second time if you stole before the tomato jumped down.

#### Joining the Clan Centurio

| **Hunt Completed** | Rogue Tomato |
| **Rewards** | 300 Gil<br>Potion (x2)<br>Teleport Stone (x1) |

Once you are in the **East Gate** area, find your friend Kytes to continue the story. After the scenes, you'll find yourself in the **North End** with notice that you can explore the lower level of the city and the world map. For now, head east and take the south exit to return to the **East End**. Directly to the east is the Sandsea. Go in to find Tomaj. Speak with him to complete the Hunt and get a hint on what to do next.

Now that that's done, you can pick up the next Hunt from the Notice Board in the Sandsea. The petitioner is conveniently also in the Sandsea. Find Gastly sitting on the ground near the tavern exit. Speak to him to formally accept and get the details on the hunt.

Exit the Sandsea and head back to the **North End** the way you came. Cross over to the west side and look for a building on the northeast corner of the intersection. Two bangaa are standing by the door. Speak to the Conspicuous Bangaa for a scene. If this is your first playthrough, talk with the occupants of the building to familiarize yourself with the Clan Centurio. When satisfied, speak to the moogle standing on the balustrade upstairs. Speak to him a second time for _Potions (x3)_. Speak to him again and he can allow you to change your job.

### Getting the Help of Old Dalan

#### Finding Old Dalan

| **Treasures** | 5 ( **South Sprawl**), 6 ( **North Sprawl**) |

Leave the Clan Hall and make your way to **South Gate.** You should be getting used to using your map by this point, but there's a tricky spot here. You can't just head south because the **West End** has no southern exit. Instead, cross the bridge to the east and head back to the **East End**, and take the southwestern most exit to return to the **Southern Plaza**.

| ##### Cartographer's Guild Moogle - _Rabanastre / Southern Plaza_ |
| **Area** | **Cost** |
| Dalmasca Estersand | 50 gil |
| Dalmasca Westersand | 550 gil |
| Giza Plains | 30 gil |

While here, find the moogle in the northwest alcove. He is from the Cartographer's Guild and sells maps of the areas. In order to get the Cartographer trophy, you need to fully explore every map. Any map you purchase from the cartographer's guild counts as fully exploring. Not only does that save you some headache in case you miss just a tiny, obscure portion, these maps are very useful when trying to navigate an unfamiliar area. Make note of the map costs and plan on making these purchases as soon as you have the gil.

Now you can exit to the south into **South Gate**. Since you've watched a lot of scenes, it's not a bad idea to use the orange Rabanastre Crystal to save. When you activate an orange crystal, it gets added to a list of locations you can fast travel to by using teleport stones. Use the doors on the east wall to enter **Lowtown** **/ South Sprawl**.

Your destination is to your left. Enter Old Dalan's Place for a scene. Afterwards, feel free to explore **Lowtown**, both the **South Sprawl** and the **North Sprawl** for items. If you recall from the PS2 version, there is a treasure chest right outside Old Dalan's Place that if you open you can never get the Zodiac Spear. That is not an issue in the PS4 version, so loot away! If you see a traveling merchant, this is a convenient time to sell the "Loot" from your inventory. You probably have rat pelts, wolf pelts, cactus fruit, pebbles, and maybe some fire stones, earth stones, wind stones, and small feathers. You may even have a Virgo Gem already. The only thing you shouldn't sell right now is your teleport stones. Check out the table below for some potential bazaar goods that you might be able to unlock and purchase with your current loot.

| **Loot to sell** | **Bazaar Good** | **Item** | **Cost** | **Savings** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cactus Fruit (x2) | Potion Pack | Potion (x2) | 70 gil | 50 gil |
| Small Feather (x3) | Tail of the Phoenix | Phoenix Down (x2) | 400 gil | 0 gil |
| Wolf Pelt (x2)<br>Earth Stone (x1) | Unassuming Surcoat | Chromed Leathers | 100 gil | 100 gil |

As you can see, you can usually save a little gil by buying from the bazaar.

Once you are satisfied with your exploring and treasure looting, head out of the **South Sprawl** the same way you entered initially to get back to the **South Gate**. If you have selected a job, you may want to return to the **East End** to buy gear. If you didn't select a job because you are waiting until you have the full party, you may want to buy some potions. You might be able to afford the maps from the cartographer moogle, too. When you're ready, head south from **Southgate** into the...

#### Giza Plains (Dry) - Old Dalan's Task

| **Treasures** | 7 ( **Throne Road**), 7 ( **Gizas North Bank**), 6 ( **Gizas South Bank**), <br>7 ( **Warrior's Wash**), 7 ( **Toam Hills**), 11 ( **Starfall Field**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Blind \[magick\], Shadestone, Sunstone |
| **Enemies** | Hyena, Giza Rabbit, Urstrix, Slaven, <br>Wildsnake, Alpha Hyena, Sleipnir, Werewolf |

You begin in the **Throne Road**. You can take some time killing Hyenas and the non-aggressive Giza Rabbits while gathering treasures, but don't bother grinding yet. Continue directly south into the **Nomad Village**. Until you introduce yourself, most of the villagers just have the name "Nomad". First speak to the nomad sitting in front of the large tent in the center of the village. Just to the west of the tent is a nomad standing next to a wooden chest. Speak to her next. Agree to her request. Speak to the nomad youth in front of you after the scenes. This will cause the next destination to be marked on your map. Afterward, if you picked a license board, there is a weapon seller just to the north where you can get level 1 weapons, armor, the bangle accessory, and some magick. Since your new party member begins with White Magick 1 unlocked and available regardless of her license board, you can save money on potions by buying the Cure spell. Save at the crystal on the southwest edge of the village before taking the west exit.

When you enter **Toam Hills**, you will get a tutorial on the party system and a small taste of the gambit system. You will also be given 3 Potions and 2 tufts of Phoenix Down. The enemies are a small step up in difficulty, but having a second party member is helpful. Keep on stealing when you can, and remember you can head back to the **Nomad Village** to the save crystal if you need to heal. There is a chest in **Toam Hills** containing the _Blind_ ability that will not reappear once opened, but it saves you from having to buy it for 200 from a magick shop. It is found in the southeastern alcove, but to get there you have to fight a Slaven followed by a group of two Hyenas accompanying an Alpha Hyena.You can also fight a hidden Wildsnake on the west side of the narrow path leading to the alcove.

Once you have explored this area to your content, proceed south into **Starfall Fields.** The Sleipnir shouldn't be too tough for you, and if you want a cheap buckler be sure to steal / win 3 moltings from it. If encountered, the Werewolves should definitely be avoided.

Make your way to the east exit into the **Crystal Glade** and talk to the boy sitting by the dark crystal. Explain the situation to continue for a scene. Once he hands over the Shadestone, there are four different shining dark crystals marked on your map you can try to absorb energy from. The amount they give differs, so it could take you anywhere from 2 to 4 attempts to charge your Shadestone. The moment you hit 100%, you'll be transported back to the boy for a couple of scenes. In addition to the Sunstone, you'll be rewarded with 50 gil, Potions (x2), and Teleport Stones (x2).

| **Loot to sell** | **Bazaar Good** | **Item** | **Cost** | **Savings** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Drab Wool (x2) | Antidote Set | Antidote (x3) | 100 gil | 50 gil |
| Molting (x3)<br>Fire Stone (x3) | Gilt Shield | Buckler | 250 gil | 250 gil |
| Large Feather (x3) | First-aid Kit | Phoenix Down (x2)<br>Potion (x2) | 450 gil | 70 gil |

Head back north to Rabanastre. If you want to do some optional material now, DO NOT GO TO LOWTOWN. Instead read on. You can complete the next two hunts and begin another side quest, or you can wait until later. If you want to do them now, read on. Otherwise, skip ahead to [The Palace Treasury](#the-palace-treasury).

### OPTIONAL Thextera and Flowering Cactoid Hunts, Patient in the Desert Sidequest

#### Wolf in the Waste

| **Treasures** | 8 |
| **Enemies** | Wolf, Thextera (mark) |

As long as you have a white magick user in your party, you should be able to take on Thextera fairly easily. To find Thextera, head to **Rabanastre / Westgate** and leave the city to access **The Dalmasca Westersand / The Galtea Downs**. Thextera can be found by following the wall on your left. You may have to fight a few Wolves on the way, and Thextera will have at least one wolf accompanying it.

> ##### Hunt: Thextera
>
>
> | **Level** | 6 | **LP** | 2 | **CP** | 300 |
> | **HP** | 380 | **Steal** | Pebble (55/80%), Potion (10/30%), Wolf Pelt (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Wind |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Confuse, Sleep, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disease, Slow, Berserk, Libra |
>
> Thextera isn't too tough as long as you have characters with over 100 HP. Eliminate the wolves around it, then pound away. It has a small chance to poison on hit, so it's wise to have some antidotes on hand. Once its health drops below 50%, it will use Cry for Help to summon another Wolf. This can be stopped by inflicting silence. It will continue to use Cry for Help regularly, so you will want to finish it off quickly. It will use Saber once after the first Cry for Help. Saber doubles its attack power at the expense of some HP. Also, its evasion and counter rates increase below 50%. Keep manually casting heal if a character drops below 100 HP. If you are doing this as soon as it is available, you may get an unlucky combo that wipes you out, but that should be rare.

| **Hunt Completed** | Thextera |
| **Rewards** | 500 Gil<br>Headguard (x1)<br>Teleport Stone (x1) |

Return to Rabanastre and back to the Sandsea to tell Gastly the good news and receive your reward. The Headguard is a little bit of an improvement over anything you can buy, if you have unlocked Light Armor 2. While you are here, check the notice board for the next hunt.

#### Dalmasca's Desert Bloom

| **Treasures** | 10 ( **The Stepping**), 4 ( **Outpost**), 9 ( **Yardang Labyrinth**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Firefly (accessory), Silence (magick), Cactus Flower, |
| **Enemies** | Cactite, Wolf, Cockatrice, Wild Saurian, Cactoid, Ripe Rampager (rare game), Flowering Cactoid (mark), |

Before finding the petitioner for this hunt, head back to the clan hall to talk with the moogle Montblanc. He will also reward you with _Warp Mote (x2)_ and _Teleport Stone (x2)_ for reaching the next rank in the clan: Hedge Knight. Make sure you have some phoenix downs and eye drops before heading out, as well. You may also consider buying up all the available techniques and magick, if you have the money. The Clan Provisioner in **Muthru Bazaar** also has Oil and Decoy for sale now. Decoy is particularly important, but costs 2000 gil. Buy it as soon as you have the free gil.

When ready to continue, do not attempt this hunt solo. Also, if you are doing the hunt early with Penelo, make sure one character has high HP and one has the ability to use white magick. If you don't, have Montblanc reset one or both of their jobs, but remember to re-equip your gear.

Take the eastern exit to access the **Yardang Labyrinth**. Before taking on the Flowering Cactoid, head toward the northeast exit. In the alcove there, you'll find a one-time chest containing the Silence spell. You may also find a chest with a Dark Mote while exploring, which will be very useful against your mark. Take the northeast exit into **Nalbina Fortress**. There isn't much to do here yet, but you can activate another teleport crystal and save before attempting to take down your mark. You may want to get a few Dark Motes for the battle ahead. You can try to farm them in **Yardang Labyrinth**, or you can go into trial mode and collect one from the chest on level 2. If you opt for trial mode, clear out the alphas that are with Thextera on level 2, claim the Dark Mote, beat Thextera, pause and quit back to menu, and reload your save file. Your Dark Mote will come with you. Then you can quit and go back to trial mode to get as many as you'd like. If you have a lot of them, you can even try level 3 by using them on the hoard of cactus enemies until only the Flowering Cactoid remains. Then try to steal the Kakarta, the rare steal and an extremely powerful weapon for a Knight, before finishing off the Flowering Cactoid. If you need strategies on beating the Flowering Cactoid, read down below where you face it to finish the hunt. When ready, head back into the **Yardang Labyrinth**.

Unlike most of the cactus-like species, the Flowering Cactoid is aggressive. That, along with the pink flower on top of its head, make it easy to identify. It's a good idea to clear out most or all of the other enemies in the area before engaging the Flowering Cactoid. It likes to run away and heal. Hope for another Dark Mote chest while clearing the enemies. After clearing the enemies, check the dead end just south of the exit to **The Outpost** to see if the rare game Ripe Rampager spawned, which you can fight now since you will need it eventually for the complete bestiary. When ready, approach the Flowering Cactoid that is usually found in the dead center of the map.

> ##### Hunt: Flowering Cactoid
>
>
> | **Level** | 4 | **LP** | 2 | **CP** | 300 |
> | **HP** | 755 | **Steal** | Cactus Fruit (55/80%), Earth Stone (10/30%), Potion (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Wind |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Sleep, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disease, Disable, Immobilize, Sap, Slow, Berserk |
>
> First, notice that this has a much higher HP than what you've seen before. On top of that, it has very high evasion, so you'll miss a lot. Most of its attacks aren't too dangerous, doing about 15-20 damage a pop, so as long as it doesn't combo you should be able to keep healed. The problem is that you may run out of MP because the fight takes so long if you are doing it early. Later in the game, it's a breeze if you have a gun.
>
> Assuming you are doing this early, if you don't kill it quickly, it will begin to run away. If it stops and you don't resume the attack immediately, it will cast cure or use a potion. Given how long it takes to remove its HP, this can be a major setback. This is why you need the enemies cleared so you don't get distracted. Below 40%, its evasion increases even higher. At any point, it has a small chance to inflict blindness, making it virtually impossible to hit. The last thing to worry about is the 1000 Needles attack which does a set 1000 damage to one party member. It only does this once after its HP drops below 20%.
>
> As soon as you see the Flowering Cactoid preparing 1000 needles, try to use your dark motes, if you have any. If you have any aero motes, even better. Tell both party members to use the motes and hope they connect to take it down before it gets the attack in. If it still gets the attack in, toss a phoenix down at the fallen party member quickly before the other party member falls and then keep whittling it down until it falls. In my attempt at level 4, Vaan was hit by 1000 needles, and Penelo (my healer) was KO'ed by a 5-hit combo right as Vaan got revived. I tossed another phoenix down and finished in just a few more hits.

| **Hunt Completed** | Flowering Cactoid |
| **Rewards** | 300 Gil<br>Potion (x10) |

Head back to **The Outpost** to talk to Dantro. He'll give you the reward along with the _Cactus Flower._ Talk to Dantro a second time and he'll mention that his wife might have something for you to do in the **South Bank Village** if you give her the flower.

#### Patient in the Desert

| **Treasures** | 9 ( **Yardang Labyrinth**), 11 ( **Sand-swept Naze**), 10 ( **Banks of the Nebra**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Poison (magick), Bundle of Needles, Semclam Shells (x5), Phials of Nebralim (x2) |
| **Enemies** | Cactite, Wolf, Cockatrice, Cactoid, Ripe Rampager (rare game), Flowering Cactoid (mark), Nekhbet (rare game), Tiny Bug, Ichthon, Greeden (rare game) |

Make your way back through the **Yardang Labyrinth**, but this time head for the northernmost exit into **Sand-swept Naze**. After you make your way down the elevated path, hug the wall to the right all the way to the southeast corner for a one-time chest with the magick Poison. After that, your destination is the northwest exit, but be cautious if you see a cockatrice with bravery or protect status. This is the rare game Nekhbet, which you probably cannot take on yet with its nearly 4000 HP and up to 12-hit combos. Instead, continue back along the edge until you can make your way north through the much more familiar Wolves and Alpha Wolf. Exit into the **Banks of the Nebra** and make your way northeast to the **South Bank Village**.

There is a Teleport Crystal that you should activate by interacting with. Dantro's wife is standing outside of her tent near the Unlucky Merchant. Hand over the Cactus Flower and speak with her again. If the quest doesn't continue, exit the village and re-enter for your next set of instructions. You can find two Semclam Shells as Mysterious Glints in the river shallows inside the village, and three in the river outside the village. The high-level Ichthons are not aggressive as long as you don't interact with them. Don't take too long finding the three shells as the high level rare game Greeden will spawn on the bank if you've been in the map for three minutes and haven't killed any cactuar-like enemies. After you've collected all 5, deliver them all at once (do not try to deliver these or any other item one at a time as it will trigger the quest to advance and lower your rewards). Next, she needs some Phials of Nebralim, which Dantro can help you with. Head back to **the Outpost** and speak with Dantro. One Mysterious Phial is on the crates piled in the center of the area. The other is on a jug beside the chocobo. Take both of these back to Dantro's Wife. Her next item is not reachable at this point in the game, so make your way back to Rabanastre.

### The Palace Treasury

#### Accessing the Palace

| **Treasures** | 6 ( **North Sluiceway**), 8 ( **North Spur Sluiceway**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Crescent Stone |
| **Enemies** | Dire Rat, Steeling, Ichthon, Razorfin (rare game) |

Once you are done with your hunts and sidequests, head to give Old Dalan the sunstone in exchange for _the Crescent Stone_. Leave **Dalan's House**. Go north to **Lowtown / North Sprawl** and make your way to the red X where the exit points to the **Garamsythe Waterway**. Open the door to **Storehouse 5**. After the scene, you'll receive _Potions (x2)_ and _phials of Eye Drops (x4)_. Open the door to the left to enter the **Garamsythe Waterway**.

Save at the crystal. East from the crystal is a very short path to a dead end and no treasures, so don't bother. Instead, head to the west and up the stairs into the next area, the **Northern Sluiceway**. There are many alcoves that might contain treasure barrels, but the path is fairly straightforward. Work your way to the east until you see the exit into the **North Spur Sluiceway**. The Ichthons here are not nearly as strong as those on the **Bank of the Nebra**, so feel free to steal from them and fight them. There is a small chance that one of the Ichthons will be replaced with the rare game Razorfin. Don't be afraid to use a potion if necessary. If you are a Shikari and have invested in HP, however, this should be a walk in the park. There are some potentially valuable treasures in this area, all near the upper walkway about two-thirds down the length of the spur. First, if you go to the dead end to the southeast of the upper walkway, if the treasure appears you have a good chance to get an aero mote. Next, just after you cross the upper walkway on the right a chest may spawn that has a good chance to have a javelin. Finally, the dead end to the southwest has a guaranteed one-time chest with a remedy. Once you've collected all the treasure you want and taken care of the enemies, proceed to the northwest into the **Palace**.

#### Raiding the Palace

| **Treasures** | 6 ( **Cellars**), 1 ( **Lower Halls**) |
| **Notable Loot** | map of the Royal Palace of Rabanastre, Goddess's Magicite |

While in the **Cellar Stores**, grab the map from the green urn behind you and use the save crystal. If you don't grab the map now, it will cost you 100,000 gil later in the game if you are going for the **Cartographer** trophy. Take the east exit into **The Cellars**. Claim all six treasures around the edges of the room (in the original this would prevent you from getting the Zodiac Spear, but there is no drawback in the Zodiac Age) for Elixir, Antidote, Remedy, Ether, Phoenix Down, and Potion. Proceed north toward the stairs to trigger a scene. Afterward, attempt to go up the stairs. Speak to the Palace Servant and follow his instructions to get around the guard. Once upstairs, proceed into the **Lower Halls**.

Your goal is to find the Lion Signet without getting caught by the guards. The guards are all stationary, but can be moved by shouting at them. If you get too close to a guard, you have to start the area over. Begin by heading straight across the first intersection until you see a hawk signet on the ground. Move past it until you have the option to shout by pressing square. Shout and run back the way you came, turning to the south at the intersection. Follow this path back to the east and take a right at the end. Hug the west wall so as not to draw the attention of the nearby guards until you reach a chest with the Orrachea Armlet accessory. Head back north to the third intersection and turn west (to the left). About halfway down the hall will be the lion signet. Activate this to light up the hidden passage.

Now you have to get all the guards on the east side. Go back to the east and head north while hugging the wall on your left. Turn left down the northernmost passage and hail the guard after moving slightly past the halfway point. Run back to the lion signet and hail the three guards to get them to chase you. Run back to the east, turn south, and turn down the next hallway to the west. Now hail the guards you tricked at the very beginning to get them back to their original position. Run back east before they catch you, turn south, and then take the last passage to the west and your way will be clear. Follow this around to the last intersection in the northwest and turn west to find the secret passage.

Interact with the Faint Light and approach the wall. In the **Secret Passage**, there is a Suspicious-looking Wall that looks like a door, but won't open. Wall-scrape the southern wall on the west end to find a Switch to press. Now examine the Suspicious-looking Wall again. After receiving the Goddess's Magicite, there's nothing to do but follow the stairs up.

#### Escaping the Palace

| **Treasures** | 4 ( **East Waterway Control**), 6 ( **No. 11 Channel**), 2 ( **Southern Sluiceway**), 2 ( **West Sluice Control**), 4 ( **No. 10 Channel**) |
| **Notable Loot** | map of the Garamsythe Waterway |
| **Enemies** | Dire Rat, Steeling, Gigantoad, Flan, Ghost, Garchimacera, Firemane (boss) |

First things first, ignore the Save Crystal and walk down the stairs. Now that that's taken care of, let's set some good gambits.

> ##### Gambit Recommendation - Initial Setup
>
> The steal gambit for Vaan may get annoying if you steal pretty successfully, so you may want to manually have him attack periodically or move him nearer a different enemy. As soon as you can, you may want to buy a "Foe: HP >90%" Gambit or something similar to help him not waste as much time.
>
> |  | **Vaan** |  | **Balthier** |  | **Fran** |
> | **Slot** | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |
> | 1 | Foe: nearest visible | Steal |  | Ally: HP <30% | Potion |  | Ally: HP <70% | Cure |
> | 2 | Foe: lowest HP | Attack |  | Foe: lowest HP | Attack |  | Foe: lowest HP | Attack |

You can also assign jobs for your new companions. If you like to stick with theme, Balthier would be a Machinist and Fran would be an Archer. While you can always redo your selections later in the Clan Hall, there is something to be said for getting all the lores to the right of the starting point for the Archer. The Machinist has good licenses in all four corners: gambits to the top left, HP to the bottom left, Swiftness and Ribbon to the bottom right, and magic and battle lores to the top right. Pick what you think will be most useful for you and head that way. Now you can use that Save Crystal.

Examine the Fallen Soldeirs at the base of the stairs then exit to the south. In the **East Waterway Control**, head immediately east to get your location area map for the **Garamsythe Waterway**. Make your way south, exploring as you desire for treasures. Do the same thing in the next section, **No. 11 Channel**. In the **East Sluice Control**, Use the Save Crystal before heading down the stairs. The four Imperial Guardsmen shouldn't be too much trouble as long as you aren't very underleveled. At levels 8 and 9, Fran never had to cast cure.

After the scene, switch Fran's #2 gambit from Attack to Fire. If Balthier or Vaan has a third gambit available, add "Ally: any » Eye Drops" in between the original two gamits for Balthier or in the top slot for Vaan. Save again before moving forward. Approach the woman you rescued to learn about "Guest" party members. Proceed west into the **Southern Sluiceway**. Before proceeding into the middle of the room, hook around to the east dead end and grab the one-time chest for a Hi-Potion. Now continue toward the west to trigger a fixed encounter.

> ##### Miniboss: Flan (x4)
>
>
> | **Level** | 5 | **LP** | 2 | **CP** | 190 |
> | **HP** | 380 | **Steal** | Potion (55/80%), Caramel (10/30%), <br>Sagittarius Gem (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Fire |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Doom, Confuse, Reverse, Sleep, Blind, Poison, Silence, Oil, Disease, Disable, Immobilize, Sap, Slow, Berserk, Libra |
>
> The fire weakness here is key, and Fran already conveniently knowing fire makes this battle easier than it probably should have been. If your gambits are set up as suggested, the only problem might come if Fran gets stuck having to cure. If that happens, you may need to adjust the gambits so that she doesn't cure until a lower HP threshold or swap restorative gambits to the men and only use potions for healing. Once Vaan has stolen from each of the Flan, you can access your gambits and switch the stealing gambit off for the rest of the encounter. The Flans can cast Cure and Blind, so if your damage output is low the battle could drag on - but just keep casting Fire and it should end quickly enough.

After the battle, find a one-time treasure barrel with a tuft of Phoenix Down in the west dead end. Proceed west into the next area and there is a chest that has a good chance to spawn in the middle of the first chamber. If it has an item, it will be a Dark Mote. You can go back into the **Southern Sluiceway** and immediately reenter and the chest can respawn with the same likelihood. This is a great place to stock up on Dark Motes if you feel like spending the time. After you have what you want, head back to the previous Save Crystal, swap Fran's gambit back to "Attack" instead of Fire, spend your license points and save.

Proceed back and through the **West Sluice Control**, fighting new enemies and getting new loot. There is a good chance for another chest with a Soleil Fang to appear in one of the western spurs. Continue north into **No. 10 Channel**. Make your way north and then east, then use the Save Crystal in the **Central Waterway Control**. Before heading through the door to the north, change the gambit that uses Eye Drops to one that uses Antidotes, and doublecheck to make sure that Fran won't be casting Fire.

> ##### Boss: Firemane
>
>
> | **Level** | 7 | **LP** | 3 | **CP** | 380 |
> | **HP** | 3571 | **Steal** | Potion (55/80%), Phoenix Down (10/30%), <br>Grimoire Togail (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Water |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Fire |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disable, Immobilize, Sap, Slow, Berserk, Libra |
>
> Firemane has some pretty strong attacks, including casting Fire and using the area attack skill Bushfire. The latter can do a lot of damage and inflict poison, so it's best to try to back up if you see Firemane ready that one. It will use it at least three times in the battle when it reaches 80%, 50%, and 20% HP; but it can use it more frequently if you take too long to finish it off. Firemane doesn't like to target anything in the water, so while you can't control your guest you can control Vaan. If Balthier and Fran are the recommended jobs, they will stay back and use ranged weapons. If done right, your guest will be the only one hit by Bushfire when it triggers, which can make a huge difference in the battle.
>
> Once Vaan has stolen from the Firemane, turn off the steal gambit. Afterward, move back into the water and start using those dark motes you farmed earlier. They still do more damage than Vaan's physical attacks at this point, so the Firemane should go down quickly.

### Getting Home

#### Escaping the Nalbina Dungeon

| **Treasures** | 4 ( **Stockade**), 3 ( **Confiscatory**), 10 ( **The Black Watch**) _\[all one-time chests\]_ |
| **Notable Loot** | map to the Nalbina Dungeons |
| **Enemies** | Daguza (boss), Galeedo (boss), Gwitch (boss), Imperial Swordsman, Imperial Marksman, Imperial Magus, Imperial Hoplite, Imperial Pilot, |

After the scenes, you will be in the **Nalbina Dungeon** **/ Stockade**, stripped of your gear. Before heading to the Save Crystal, go around and find the four treasure urns. Each contains a Knot of Rust that will come in very handy soon - particularly if you are low-level. One can be found to the left at the stairs just outside of the room where you awake. One is in the southwest corner of the main area, and the last two are in the southeast corner. Save your game at the crystal in the north and then proceed to **The Arena**. After the scene, you'll find yourself engaged with three seeq, Daguza, Galeedo, and Gwitch. If you have time, you can attempt to steal from them, but none of them have anything special. You can take them out very quickly by tossing Knots of Rust at them. While they do give Clan Points on beating them and can thus be considered bosses, the battle is too effortless to require a full strategy. At level 9, Balthier took them all out on his own without dropping to 50% health.

After some more scenes, you'll find yourself in the **Confiscatory**. Move forward toward the Save Crystal to trigger another scene where you get your gear back and a location map for the area. There are three treasures here as well, but one of them causes the Zodiac Spear to not appear in the original game. If you are playing Zodiac Age, you can safely grab all three. If you aren't, this guide is probably already confusing. The chests contain another Orrachea Armlet, 428 gil, and a Tourmaline Ring. Now feel free to save.

Continue to the east. The next area is composed of two large rooms. There are multiple one-time chests scattered throughout. Take your time and wipe out all of the Imperials for the experience, LP, and drops and watch the nooks for treasures. If you trigger the scene showing the judges opening the door, don't feel like you have to follow immediately. If you need to explore more, you may. When ready, go to the center of the north wall in the north room to find the exit.

#### Keeping the Lights on in Barheim

| **Treasures** | 3 ( **The Lightworks**), 3 ( **OP Sector 29**), 7 ( **Great Eastern Passage**), 3 ( **OPS Sector 36**), 3 ( **Special OP Sector 3**), 5 ( **OP Sector 37**), 4 ( **Great Central Passage**), 9 ( **The Zeviah Subterrane**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Fuse Tube, map of the Barheim Passage |
| **Enemies** | Battery Mimic, Zombie, Steeling, Seeker, Tiny Battery, Flan, Bomb, Specters, Skull Defenders, Mimic, Tiny Mimic, Suriander, Mimic Queen (boss) |

Before going to the Save Crystal on the left, there may be a couple of chests (including a guaranteed one-time chest with gil) to grab in the alcove to the right. On your way back west toward the Save Crystal, take the left toward the column in the center of the room and examine the Power Relay for a scene. Skip the Save Crystal and head downstairs. There is a merchant Burrogh that will hand over the _Tube_ _Fuse_. He also gives you your first chance to purchase new gambits. If you can afford it (~2,000 gil), buy all of them. Now would be a good time to change Vaan's gambit for stealing to "Foe: HP = 100%" or "Foe: HP ≥ 70%". You should probably put a gambit on someone that targets "Self" with "Libra". Be sure to put it at the bottom of the list as you don't want them interrupting battle to activate Libra. You can also set up Basch's gambits if you like (perhaps to also target "Foe: lowest HP"). If you sell off all the loot you've collected, you should get a few thousand gil and unlock at least one item.

| **Loot to sell** | **Bazaar Good** | **Item** | **Cost** | **Savings** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Demon Eyeball (x2) | Eye Drop Set | Eye Drops (x3) | 100 gil | 50 gil |

You can buy up the best available weapons and armor based on your licenses, too, or you can hold out and hope what you're looking for will be available through the bazaar or treasure chest soon. It's unlikely that you'll unlock any gear from the bazaar at this point. The Mage Masher and Capella, however, will be available in treasure chests in the middle and toward the end of the Barheim Passage respectively. If you don't mind fighting with current gear for a while (and don't mind zoning and respawning the chests a few times in case you don't get what you're looking for the first time), you can save a bit of money by holding out.

With shopping done, you can press the Gate Switchboard next to Burrogh, but nothing will happen until you install the Tube Fuse upstairs in the Power Relay. Do that, then finally use the Save Crystal. Now the switch next to Burrogh is functional and will open the gate. Press it and head south into **OP Sector 29**. After the scene, take out the Battery Mimic and Zombie. If you are lower level and struggling to defeat enemies quickly, focus fire on the battery mimic. They are weak to ice, if you have a magic caster. Once cleared, look quickly for treaures before heading into the next room and downstairs to take out another battery mimic. If you have a magic user, one of the chests that might spawn may contain a Pointy Hat, if you don't already have one. After you've scoured for treasure, head east into the **Great Eastern Passage**.

Immediately upon entering you'll see a battery mimic draining the power. Take it out and head south. Follow the fork to the right to deal with another battery mimic. Head back to the fork and go south until you come to another fork. This time head left. At the end of this path is another battery mimic. Finish it off and head back to take the right path. When you come to the next intersection, head straight north to find the map urn for the area, then make your way south until you reach a platform with an exit and an energy conduit. Take out the battery mimic and head into the **OPS Sector 36** accessed from the platform.

There are two battery mimics to be dealt with in here and then you can grab some treasure. One conduit is on the upper level near where you entered, the other is down the stairs. Of the three chests that spawn in the area, one spawns on the lower level around the central column and may hold a Buckler. Upstairs past the entrance may be another chest with a Rod. At the end of the path upstairs is a one-time guaranteed chest with a Battle Harness, an accessory that lets the wearer counterattack. This would be useful for Vaan if the others are still using ranged weapons.

Head back to the **Great Eastern Passage** and this time take the south exit. Chase down the Battery Mimic to the energy conduit and you'll notice that a gate blocks you from going further south. After finishing the Battery Mimic here, head toward the western chamber. From here forward, Flans will surprise your party by dropping from the ceiling. Don't forget that they are weak to fire, but are not much stronger than [when you faced them](#walkthrough-part-1---the-occupied-city) in the **Garamsythe Waterway.** After passing through the first room, there is another Battery Mimic and conduit in the westernmost room. Deal with it and the frequent Flan drops and then make your way upstairs to the west exit into **OP Sector 37**.

This is the sector where you can find a Mage Masher, but it is the chest furthest from the entrance. Once you clear the area, if you don't get it, you'll have to keep making the hike to the exit and re-enter to respawn the chest and hope for better luck. Your first order of business is to take out the battery mimic just to the left of where you enter the area. If you continue to the north, you'll have to deal with a lot of Flan drops and no access to the next battery mimic. Instead, head down the stairs and make your way to the east hallway. When you see the Battery Mimic, take it out and now you can deal with the Flans as you need without power being drained. Head west from the second energy conduit and take the stairs at the end up to a platform with a Gate Switch. You'll have to deal with Flans, but once you do you can activate the switch to raise the gate blocking your way south back in **Special OP Sector 3**. At the east end of the platform is the chest that _might_ contain the Mage Masher. Head back to the entrance to the area. If you want, you can now go past the energy conduit to the platform that didn't access the gate switch. There will be a couple of Flan drops if you're needing the experience, and a couple of possible chest spawns including one with a 70% chance of holding an ether. Most of the chests in this area have the chance of holding gear: Buckler, Pointy Hat, Oak Staff, and Mage Masher. So if you need any of those, keep trying until you get it.

Head out of **OP Sector 37** and south out of **Special OP Sector 3**. If the Bombs spawned in **Special OP Sector 3**, you can take them out if you want or leave them alone. They aren't too tough if you are level 8 or 9 and are weak to water. Save your game in the **North-South Junction**.

Head south into the **Great Central Passage**. Take out the Battery Mimic straight south then get your power back up. Several of the chests in this area will turn out to be Mimics. You may notice that Basch has gotten quite the power up since before the Save Crystal. In the next area, if the power drops below 50% you might encounter Skeletons and Skull Defenders. If you find yourself overwhelmed, find the Battery Mimics quickly and then get back to the save crystal. There are quite a few possible items to pick up in the **Zeviah Subterrane** as well, including Aquara Mote, Ether, Cypress Pole, Iron Hammer, Lightning Fang, Balance Mote, Cherry Staff, Capella, and Dark Mote. So if you find yourself wanting any of those, be sure to clear out the area and then respawn the chests until you get what you're looking for. Tiny Mimics and Suriander may respawn as well, but not the Battery Mimics. Now that you're prepared, head south into the **Zeviah Subterrane**.

There are three Battery Mimics to deal with, so power can get drained fairly quickly. Your first probably gets targeted the moment you step into the area, and the second is shortly ahead on the left. The last requires you heading south and then turning left to head back north. You'll find it just before turning to the southeast toward the exit. Once those are cleared, you can explore the area at your leisure for treasure. If you're looking for the Capella, head to the southernmost portion of the region and head west. The first chest you might encounter could contain the Cherry Staff, but the Capella would be in the chest past the second Suriander after crossing where the path is partially submerged by water. Use whichever exit you want to respawn chests. When ready, use the Save Crystal in the next section before heading south into **Terminus 4** and your next boss battle. If you struggle with the battle, keep in mind that you can always keep fighting in the **Barheim Passage**. You can also turn on the 2X or 4X speed if you really want to make some progress.

> ##### Boss: Mimic Queen
>
>
> | **Level** | 10 | **LP** | 5 | **CP** | 410 |
> | **HP** | 4073 | **Steal** | Knot of Rust (55/80%), Storm Magicite (10/30%), <br>Rose Corsage (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Ice |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Lightning |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Confuse, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disable, Sap, Slow, Berserk, Libra |
>
> You may want to change your attack gambits before entering the battle to target "Foe: Highest Max HP". There will be four Tiny Mimics when the battle starts, and if you kill any the Mimic Queen will heal herself with Breath of Life and then use Spawn to generate another Tiny Mimic. Instead, focus fire on the Mimic Queen. Blizzard does a lot of damage, but she unleashes some powerful attacks when her health gets low so be sure not to use up all of the MP of whoever is casting Cure. Shockstorm can do over 150 damage and Ground Shaker can do near 100 damage. Both can hit multiple targets. If you have to, get any fallen party members back up quickly and finish her off before she cycles back around and uses the commands again. She has a low chance of using them when her health is above 20%, but they start coming more frequent after she falls below 20%. Just keep up the pressure, avoid killing the Tiny Mimics so she won't heal, and she'll fall eventually.

#### Home Stretch

You can talk to one of the Seeqs after you escape from Barheim to get a clue to a later sidequest, but there isn't anything else to do except save and start making your way back to Rabanastre. If you want to attempt the rare game Nekhbet to get it added to the bestiary, kill some cockatrice once you enter the **Sand-swept Naze**. Each one you kill has a chance to spawn Nekhbet somewhere in the area. If you have 300+ HP on Basch and a healing gambit on someone, you should be able to take it out fairly easily. It may take a few minutes because Nekhbet has almost as much HP as the Mimic Queen. Once done, head back to Rabanastre via **Yardang Labyrinth**, **Outpost**, and **The Stepping**, racking up LP as you go by killing all the foes in your path. After you enter Rabanastre, there is a scene and now Vaan is solo again.

First thing's first, talk to the moogle Hurdy near the Save Crystal to learn how to use the moogling. This will accelerate your movement through the city. Don't forget to save.

If you want to get some quick experience and loot, there is a rare game called Dustia that spawns in the **Westersand / Corridor of Sand** when a party member has less than 10% HP. It is undead and can be killed instantly with a phoenix down (keep in mind that you can toggle your target between party and foes using L1 and R1). By entering the **Corridor of Sand** from **Galtea Downs** with HP already lowered (by attacking yourself), Dustia will spawn right next to the entrance. If you time it right, you can grab any loot it drops and exit the area before the exp and LP shows up on the screen. If you do, you can reenter immediately and spawn it again to build up a chain. Be sure to have a good stock of phoenix downs if you plan to attempt this. The advantage of doing it now is that there is nobody to split the experience with, so Vaan will gain more levels - consequently increasing the levels of every party member from now on who joins. If you need help with the timing, there are good YouTube videos that show how it's done. For reference, I only did a few levels to make sure I understood how it worked, and I stopped at level 12 to prevent getting to overpowered at this point in the game. Do what you think you'd enjoy.

Another option that is less efficient is using the rare game Fideliant. This undead foe spawns near the center of **Dalmasca Westersand** **/ The Midfault**. Like Dustia, it can be killed with a Phoenix Down, making for very fast experience; but since it spawns near the center of the zone it can't be used repeatedly. You have to backtrack into **Rabanastre** to respawn it once it is killed.

Once you're ready, head to Muthru Bazaar and find Gastly. He should be a little north of the Clan Provisioner, standing on the west wall. After that, you should have a lot of loot to sell. Dependent on your luck and the enemies you fought in **Barheim**, the following items may be available:

| **Loot to sell** | **Bazaar Good** | **Item** | **Cost** | **Savings** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bat Fang (x1)<br>Rat Pelt (x2)<br>Dark Stone (x2) | Bow and Bodkin | Parallel Arrows<br>Shortbow | 400 gil | 450 gil |
| Iron Scraps (x3)<br>Foul Flesh (x2)<br>Earth Stone (x3) | Iron-forged Blade | Iron Sword | 650 gil | 600 gil |
| Fish Scale (x2)<br>Green Liquid (x2)<br>Dark Stone (x3) | Marksman's Delight | Silent Shot<br>Capella | 550 gil | 750 gil |

Head over to the Clan Hall to get rewards from Montblanc for your accomplishments so far. For the Flans you receive _150 gil_, for defeating Firemane you are rewarded _200 gil_, and for taking out the Mimic Queen he gives you _300 gil_. While talking to Montblanc, ask about any new hunts and take on the Cluckatrice and Rocktoise hunts. We won't be in a rush to finish these hunts yet, as they are a bit challenging currently.

Next, you have the chance to get an item of your choosing by talking to a Viera that needs advice. The items you could earn depend on how you answer her questions. She has four questions and only asks the next one after a certain game event has passed. Until you've answered all her questions and progressed the game to the point of receiving your reward, she will be in **Muthru Bazaar** on the east side of the stairs leading up to **North End**. Once you've answered all her questions, leave **Rabanatsre** and return for your reward. If she hasn't moved, you'll need to advance th story a little further. If you earned the Ether and Hi-Potions, she could now appear in the same location, in **North End** across from the Gambit Shop, in **North End** near the **Muthru Bazaar** exit, or the **Aerodrome** accessible from **Westgate**. Below is a table of how to answer each question to get the item you want and where to find Ktjn to claim your reward.

| ##### **Ktjn's Questions**<br> _\[timing\]_ | **Item »**<br>**_Ktjn's Location_** | **Runeblade** _Inside Clan HQ_ | **Hunting Crossbow** _In front of Clan HQ_ | **Fuzzy Miter** _Inside Migelo's Shop_ | **Firefly** _Outside Migelo's Shop_ | **Ether, Hi-Potion (x2)** _Somewhere in Rabanastre_ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Is the city not wonderful? ... But the land ... Should this not trouble me? <br>_\[After defeating Mimic Queen\]_ | Yes, you should be ashamed. | Yes, you should be ashamed. | No, don't be silly. | No, don't be silly. | No, don't be silly. |
| I knew my choice would bring hardship, yet...<br>_\[ [After Bujherba](#walkthrough-part-2---seeding-the-revolution)\]_ | You should've thought it through. | You should've thought it through. | Things will get better. | Things will get better. | You should've thought it through. |
| All this time my sister ... I would be as she is. Do you think it possible?<br>_\[ [After](#walkthrough-part-3---forging-an-alliance) Tomb of Raithwall\]_ | It'll never happen. | I don't know your sister | Sure, if you put your mind to it. | I don't know your sister. | _Any response._ |
| Still, not on kindness alone ... What do you think?<br>_\[ [After Mt. Bur-Omisace](#walkthrough-part-4---infiltrating-the-empire)\]_ | I think it's a good idea. | I think it's a good idea. | I am not sure. | I am not sure. | _Any response._ |

The Runeblade would be my choice since the rest are all available long before you finish the quest, but go for what you want. I'll post reminders and a link back here when the next question becomes available. To return to your location, use the links in the table. To get this started, talk to Ktjn twice on the stairs.

Another important thing to do before progressing with the story is to unlock all the Grimoires in the Bazaar, even though you can't afford them and probably don't want any yet. First head to the Sandsea and read the Hunt Notice Board 40 times. Technically, you shouldn't need to do this more than 38 times since you've already looked at it at least twice for previous hunts. Pick up the Wraith hunt while you're at it - another hunt that we will wait on until we at least have a full party. Leave the Sandsea and head to the Magick shop. There should be new Bazaar goods, so check to make sure there are 3 Forgotten Grimoires available. If not, go back and interact with the Hunt Notice Board a few more times. Now, back out and talk to the magick shop owner repeatedly until a new bazaar good becomes available. It should take between 20 and 25 attempts. Next head south to the armor shop and talk to the owner about 15 times until the next bazaar good becomes available. It might be good to check and make sure that none of the Forgotten Grimoires cost 25000 right now. If so, you may need to talk to the armor merchant or magick store owner a few more times. It would be really surprising, however, if that happened as it meant you had already spoken with merchants about 30 more times than I had for no really good reason this early in the game. Next, do the same thing with the weapon seller, except now keep talking to him until bazaar goods become available twice. This may take a while (about 20 to 30 for the first time). These Forgotten Grimoires improve the drops of different classes of creatures. There is no need to rush to get these as they tend to only cause higher level creatures to drop new loot while everything else starts dropping pebbles (which sell for 2 gil). You will eventually want to get the one costing 18,000 gil before fighting Worgens, and that will probably be your first one to purchase. I'll let you know when a good purchase time for each one is so that you have it in time for the monsters that have improved loot drops.

This is also the earliest point in the game where you can get the ultimate bow Seitengrat on the deck of the leisure craft between Rabanastre and Nalbina. Be forewarned that the likelihood of spawning this invisible (yes, you don't even get an exclamation mark over your head when next to it) chest is extremely small, and the likelihood of it containing the Seitengrat is also very small - so if you desperately want it you should check out a guide that shows you how to manipulate the random number generator. This bow does not need a license and will make the game completely unchallenging. I'll be writing the guide assuming you did not get the bow. If you do go for the bow, you can kick off another sidequest when you are ready to finish the flight. Talk to the chief steward who seems to have someone bothering her. Investigate by asking who Rande is. After some dialogue, you'll be given Ann's Letter. You can't finish this sidequest yet, but it's worthwhile getting it initiated now.

#### Welcome Home

| **Notable Loot** | Sword of the Order |

It's time to move the story forward. Head to Migelo's Shop for a scene. You can buy phoenix downs, now, if you decide you want to farm Dustia for a while. The quickest way to Old Dalan is through the entrance to **Lowtown** in **Southgate**. Before finishing Dalan's task, now's a good time to go and speak to the petitioner for the Wraith hunt. Head north from Old Dalan's as far as you can in the **North Sprawl**. Speak to Milha, standing outside a private residence.

If you are trying to use every job, making Basch a Knight makes sense (if Vaan is a Shikari). If you managed to steal the Karkata earlier from the Flowering Cactoid in Trial Mode, you can make a beeline for it on the license board by following the path all the way around to the south west corner of the eastern arm. Basch will be lacking pretty much all of the beneficial licenses, but when he gets to use the Karkata it will pay off. I was about 580 LP short of reaching the Karkata at this point (I had 500 to spend).

Make your way back up into Rabanastre proper and head toward the **Sandsea** for another scene. Go inside and find your sky pirate friends upstairs. Leave the **Sandsea** and head north to **Yamoora's Gambits**. If you already purchased the available magick and techniks in Barheim, then gambits are the only new thing to purchase. Be sure to get at least "Self: MP<10%", "Ally: Status=KO", and "Foe: Flying". These are very useful, and pick up anything else you think you might want.

### OPTIONAL Wraith and Cluckatrice Hunts

#### Waterway Haunting

Before leaving town, we can now take on the Wraith Hunt. Head across the street from the gambit shop to enter **Lowtown** and head over to the **Garamsythe Waterway**. Be sure to bring at least one healer in the party and some Phoenix Downs just in case.

> ##### Hunt: Wraith
>
>
> | **Level** | 9 | **LP** | 5 | **CP** | 750 |
> | **HP** | 5146 | **Steal** | Glass Jewel (55/80%), Dark Magicite (10/30%), <br>Hi-Potion (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Lightning |
> | **Elemental Resistance** | \[Absorb\] Ice |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Stop, Confuse, Reverse, Blind, Silence, Oil, Disable, Immobilize, Sap, Slow, Berserk |
>
> Wraith doesn't have much to be concerned about as long as you aren't solo. It casts Doom, which is a slow countdown to KO. If someone falls, be sure to bring that party member back with a Phoenix Down before the others fall too. If you are taking this on after picking up Fran and Balthier, you should have a backup party member in case the others fall. It can also cast Blizzard, Blind, and Dark and has Poison Touch and Sleep Touch. If a status connects that is preventing you from continuing the damage output, try to deal with it. The Wraith should fall fairly quickly.

| **Hunt Completed** | Wraith |
| **Rewards** | 500 Gil<br>Ether (x1)<br>Gauntlets (x1) |

Once the Wraith has been disposed of, make your way back to Milha a little further east to collect your reward.

#### Little Love on the Big Plain

Now head down to the **Nomad Village** on the **Giza Plains**. The petitioner for the Cluckatrice hunt is Dania, who can be found in the cockatrice pen. Speak with her to accept the hunt.

In all honesty, I would recommend saving this hunt for later as it is very challenging this early - but this is the earliest chance you'd get. Hopefully you are stocked up on aquara motes from **Barheim** and at least one person in your party has Remedy Lore 2 unlocked on the License Board.
