I also took this opportunity to craft the Tournesol and the Kumbha. It took some time farming Serpent Eyes and Hell's-gate Flames, but if you want the crafting details check out the [Ultimate Weapon](#optional---endgame-sidequests) section.

### Fishing Sidequest

Why bother fishing? This is an extremely long sidequest that eventually gets you one of the pieces of loot needed for the Wyrmhero Blade. You can also get the rarer loot Adamantite and Scarletite during later stages. It is best to wait until after you have completed the hunt for Gilgamesh. Note that fishing is not tied to any trophies, so if you are just trying to get Platinum, you can completely skip this. If you want to own every piece of gear, I hope you enjoy this mini-game. It is very reminiscent of calibrating the lightning towers in Final Fantasy X-2.

To kick things off, take the Muramata fishing pole you found in Balfonheim Port to Ruksel in **Dalmasca Estersand / South Bank Village**. Talk to him a second time to start fishing. You can ask how it works, if you need. A set of commands will scroll across the screen and you have to input them correctly before it reaches the edge. Since the movement is not smooth, it can be a little difficult - particularly distinguishing between circle and square. All of the areas except Downstream will scroll inputs vertically and can use the shoulder buttons (L1, L2, R1, and R2), giving you much less time to input more complicated commands. Each fish type has a corresponding set of commands, so after time you will get practiced and the area will start to seem easier. It is very frustrating when you first move to a new area, but be patient with yourself.

Initially, you only have one location to fish: Downstream. Other locations can be unlocked as described in the table below.

| **Location** | **Input Length** | **Rewards** | **Unlock Conditions** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Downstream | 2-4 | - 100 gil<br>- Hi-Potion<br>- Water Stone | Initial location |
| Midstream | 4-5 | - Fish Scale<br>- 100 gil<br>- Water Magicite | Catch all 6 fish Downstream 5 times in a row |
| Upstream | 4-5 | - Fish Scale<br>- Turtle Shell<br>- Frogspawn<br>- Cactoid Compact (once, requires Matamune) | Catch all 6 fish Midstream 5 times |
| Hidden Shoals | 5-6 | - Hi-Potion<br>- Adamantite<br>- Cactoid Commendation (once) | Fish Upstream with Matamune and receive Cactoid Compact reward |
| Den of the River Lord | 6 or 8 | - Adamantite<br>- Scarletite | Receive Cactoid Commendation as reward from Hidden Shoals |

To get the Matamune, you have to first defeat Gilgamesh, one of the Elite Marks. Once you do, the Empty Bottles may turn out to be Colored Bottles appearing in your Key Items. There are five colored bottles to collect: Blue, Green, Red, Yellow, and Black. Each one has a coded message. You'll be notified each time you get one at the end of the trip. If you like puzzles, I'll keep the key to the puzzle, the solution, and the action to take hidden so you can reveal one at a time if you get stumped.

| Color Bottle | Coded Message | Key | Solution | Action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Blue | Truth lies just beyond falsehood.<br>_CDZCKZMCRANZS_ | Advance each letter by one in the alpabet | _DEADLANDSBOAT_ | Inspect the boat in the southeast corner of **Nabreus Deadlands / Echoes of the Past**. |
| Green | Skip on stones to far bank's shore, by skipping stones return once more.<br>_SSADLNIAKLADHAUETDNREA_ | Write out each letter when you skip every other one, then do the same in reverse | _SALIKAHUTNEARDEADLANDS_ | Inspect the hut in **The** **Salikawood / Piebald Path**. |
| Red | Foothills rise in mountain's shadow.<br>_nhIiQsTUrEe_ | Unscramble the capital letters and the lowercase letters as two separate words | _QUIETshrine_ | In the northwest area of **Mosphoran Highwaste / Babbling Vale**, inspect the Quiet Shrine. |
| Yellow | Dragons stir among the eaters of the wind<br>1 2 - - 5 - 7 - 9 - | Eaters of wind use wind for energy | The numbers correspond to windmills on **Cerobi Steppe** | In **Cerobi Steppe**, turn on windmills 1 and 2 in **North Liavell Hills**, 5 in **Terraced Bank**, 7 in **Crossfield**, and 9 in **The Northsward**. Make sure the rest are off. |
| Black | Easy as a, b, c, so count yourself lucky.<br>19-12-21-9-3-5-7-1-20-5-18-1-20-19 | Each numbers corresponds to a letter in that position in the alphabet | SLUICEGATERATS | In **Garamsythe Waterway / Central Waterway Control**, follow the scurring rats on the south side to the place where they gather around a glowing object. Inspect it. |

Each bottle will react as you take each action, giving you another clue.

**Blue bottle** \- receive an X-Potion and Wanly Limned Message: _"River...unde...o"_

**Green bottle** \- receive a Hastega Mote and a Green-hued Message: _"Forest...rgro...w"_

**Red bottle** \- receive a Holy Mote and a Vermillion Message: _"Fire...undb...h"_

**Yellow bottle**\- receive a Hi-Ether and an Auric Message: _"Ash...ridg...er"_

**Black bottle** \- receive an Elixir and an Onyx Message: _"Darkness...eton...e"_

Key1: Order the first words in logical order.

Solution to Key1: River, Forest, Fire, Ash, Darkness

Key2: Read the middle portion in the order determined, followed by the last portion in the order determined.

Solution to Key2: "undergroundbridgetonowhere"

Key3: An underground location that has a collapsed bridge.

Solution to Key3: There is a portion of **Barheim Passage / Zeviah Span** that can only be accessed from **West Annex**.

When you reach that location, you will be greeted by a ... familiar ... laugh? Congratulations on receiving the Matamune. Ruksel will pleased. You will now be able to win the Cactoid Compact (10% chance) for catching all 6 fish in one trip. Once you do, you will have access to the Hidden Shoals. There you will need to win the Cactoid Commendations to reach Den of the River Lord.

Once you progress all the way to Den of the River Lord, you will need to catch the King of the Nebra 10 times to receive the Lu Shang's Badge. You can catch it multiple times per trip and you do not have to catch every fish for it to count. The commands for the King of the Nebra are a string of 8, so it may be good to practice a bit:

|     |     |     |     |     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R1 | L2 | ↑ | ◻ | R1 | L1 | X | → |

When you sell the Lu Shang's Badge along with the badges won from defeating Yiazmat and Omega Mark XII, the Wyrmhero Blade will unlock in the Bazaar. You can also fish for adamantite and scarletite if you need any for crafting, but other than that there is nothing left to do with fishing.

### Clan Centurio - Completing the Regular Marks

At this point, you probably have two active hunts: [Ixion](#optional---endgame-sidequests) and the [Shadowseer](#optional---endgame-sidequests). At a Notice Board, you should be able to grab all of the remaining non-Elite Marks: [Deathgaze](#optional---endgame-sidequests), [Diabolos](#optional---endgame-sidequests), [Piscodaemon](#optional---endgame-sidequests), [Wild Malboro](#optional---endgame-sidequests), [Catoblepas](#optional---endgame-sidequests), [Fafnir](#optional---endgame-sidequests), and [Pylraster](#optional---endgame-sidequests). Grab them all. If you've already defeated Gilgamesh, these probably won't be too challenging. They will, however, get you a few more levels before taking on the Elites or any remaining espers. Also, there are only two more marks to be tasked with, but most or all of these have to be dealt with to get Montblanc to offer the last two: Behemoth King and ultimately [Yiazmat](#optional---endgame-sidequests).

#### Paying for the Past

Teleport to **Jahara** and find War-chief Supinelo in the south section of **Lull of the Land**.

There are a couple of routes to reach the mark. The simpler is to enter **Zertinan Caverns** from the **Ozmone Plain**. Head north through **Halls of Ardent Darkness** and **The Undershore** into **Athroza Quicksands**. Take the eastern exit into **Hourglass Basin** and head south into the portion of **The Undershore** that has the Save Crystal. A bit to the south of the Save Crystal the sand makes it difficult to see, but there is a hidden path that contains a one-time treasure that has a 25% spawn rate with Thief's Cuffs. This hidden path also leads to the eastern section of **Hourglass Basin**. You will find the mark in the back of this zone.

> ##### Hunt: Catoblepas
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 27 | **CP** | 5940 |
> | **HP** | 187991 | **Steal** | Hi-Potion (55/80%), Beastlord Hide (10/30%), Gemini Gem (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Dark<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Sleep, Blind, Slow, Libra |
>
> The Catoblepas is primarily physical attacks. They can be pretty damaging to an underprepared party, but a tank with armor for Heavy Armor 10 or 11 and Protect status reduces the damage to only a few hundred. It may be challenging to keep Supinelo alive throughout the battle, but Protect and Bubble help.
>
> Start the battle with Dispel, and use it again if Catoblepas restores its Protect status. Near the end of the battle, it will use Wail. Remove any bad statuses that it causes and finish it off.

| **Hunt Completed** | Catoblepas |
| **Rewards** | 3200 Gil<br>Volcano (x1)<br>Arctic Wind (x1) |

On your way back through the west side of **Hourglass Basin**, you should run into the rare game Molen. Return to **Jahara / Lull of the Land** to find War-chief Supinelu to claim your reward. The Arctic Wind you receive can be sold to craft Feathered Boots in the bazaar, which are the Winged Boots accessory but 200 gil cheaper than what it costs at the merchant.

#### The Black Sorcerer

Make your way to **Mt Bur-Omisace / Temple Grounds**. Shortly before you reach the stairs leading to the **Hall of Light**, there is a named acolyte Ivaness. Speak to him to hear details regarding the hunt for Piscodaemon.

If you wish, you can also speak to the petitioner for the Fafnir hunt, but beware that Fafnir is much tougher than your other marks have been so far with over one million HP. If you want to initiate that quest, speak to Relj in **Temple Approach**.

Finding the Black Sorcerer hunt will require you traveling deep into **Giruvegan**. The mark is located on the highest level of **Gate of Fire**, reached by heading west after entering the area from **The Haalmikah Water-Steps**.

> ##### Hunt: Piscodaemon
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 26 | **CP** | 4730 |
> | **HP** | 49660 | **Steal** | Potion (55/80%), Reflectga Mote (10/30%), White Robes (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Immobilize, Slow, Libra |
>
> This mark has much fewer HP than most of your recent marks. As such, it should go down very quickly. It's best to Dispel it at the beginning of the battle and take out any nearby foes. Equip Black Belts to prevent Disable and perhaps have a Remedy gambit set up just in case. It is not much stronger than the regular foes in Giruvegan, so if you were able to reach it without problem you should be able to defeat it without much more.

| **Hunt Completed** | Piscodaemon |
| **Rewards** | 3800 Gil<br>Dark Shot (x1)<br>Scathe Mote (x1) |

Hike back to the Teleport Crystal and return to Ivaness in **Mt Bur-Omisace / Temple Grounds** to get your reward.

#### Visitor on Deck

To start of this hunt, visit the **Aerodrome** in **Rabanastre**. Look for a child wearing a white cloak over a maroon tunic. Before you speak with him, he'll be labelled "Traveler".

After accepting the hunt, talk to the boy again and he'll tell you, "I didn't see it last time". Book a leisure craft flight to some other location. On each flight, it's possible Deatgaze will appear. If it doesn't, find and talk to the boy at the next stop. This time hell conclude by saying, "It was quite a ways off." Book another flight and if Deathgaze doesn't appear find the boy at the next location. He should wrap up by saying, "I felt the ship shake last time...it must've been close!" Do this again for another location and he'll say, "I heard it roar last time...it must've been close!" On your next flight, you'll get asked for volunteers to fight Deathgaze. Agree to and head into the Observation Parlor and up to the Air Deck. Deathgaze will be up the stairs on the highest level of the Air Deck.

> ##### Hunt: Deathgaze
>
>
> | **Level** | 50 | **LP** | 28 | **CP** | 7560 |
> | **HP** | 184000 | **Steal** | Phoenix Down (55/80%), Charger Barding (10/30%), Emperor Scale (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Earth |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Wind |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Reverse, Silence, Sap |
>
> The best protection you can have during this fight is equipping the Sash accessory. It will halve the damage of the Fireball - which if you follow my instructions is one of only two attacks of concern. The other is Crushing Fangs which can be an instant KO for one person, so be ready with an Arise gambit.
>
> To make this battle easier, there are a couple of status susceptibilities you want to take advantage of. Have a black magick user set up with gambits to cast Silence first and then Bio repeatedly. This will accomplish a couple of things. First, it will prevent Deathgaze from using all its high level magick attacks that are very dangerous in these close quarters. Second, Bio does good damage and inflicts Sap, so Deathgaze's HP will constantly drain. As long as it has its physical paling up, be sure to only attack with magick or Shades of Black technick. When it puts up a magick paling, be sure to switch you black magick user out for a physical fighter or change the gambit to attack instead of Bio. As long as your white magick user is doing okay keeping Shell, Regen, and Curaja going, you should have no problem. If the MP runs low consistently, consider using up one of your Hi-Ethers.

| **Hunt Completed** | Deathgaze |
| **Rewards** | 3400 Gil<br>Elixir (x2) |

#### A Wild Stench

The petitioner for this hunt is in **Eruyt Villaga / The Spiritwood**. Make your way to the northwest area of the zone to find Rena pacing slowly.

The mark shows up where you faced Rafflesia in **The Feywood / Redolent Glade**. The fastest way there is to teleport to **Giruvegan** and bactrack through **The Feywood**. Alternatively, you can hike there from where you are now as it isn't much further.

> ##### Hunt: Wild Malboro
>
>
> | **Level** | 50 | **LP** | 28 | **CP** | 3960 |
> | **HP** | 110842 | **Steal** | Malboro Flower (55/80%), Foul Liquid (10/30%), Virgo Gem (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Wind |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Silence, Oil, Slow, Libra |
>
> This is a powerful Malboro. Expect status ailments. Remedy and/or Esuna gambits are a must. Don't have gambits that cure one status at a time. Keeping party members spread apart is a must (which means ranged weapons or magick for at least two. Take advantage of the weakness to wind and set up a black magick user with a Cloud Staff (or Staff of the Magi, if you have it already) and a gambit to cast Aeroga. If you have it, also keep Faith status on your Aeroga caster. You can also cast Bravery on your physical attacker, but you might want to have them equipped with a Bowline Sash to prevent Confuse status. Shell on everyone is good to help resist bad statuses.
>
> There will be at least three other Malboros accompanying the Wild Malboro, but Aeroga should take care of them quickly. In fact, the buffed Aeroga did so much damage that I finished off Wild Malboro in only a few shots (Aeroga was doing 15-20k damage and my Knight/Bushi was doing around 8k damage with the Tournesol). It may manage to cast Renew and fully restore its HP, after which it will put up a physical paling preventing physical damage. Aeroga will still get through and the battle will be over shortly.

| **Hunt Completed** | Wild Malboro |
| **Rewards** | 4600 Gil<br>Euclid's Sextant (x1) |

Return to Rena either by hiking through **The Feywood** and **Golmore Jungle**, or returning to the Giruvegan Crystal to teleport back to **Eruyt Village**. She will express her gratitude with the standard reward: gil and gear.

#### Who's the Strongest?

This hunt is one that may be a step up from the last few. With nearly half a million HP, there aren't many foes you've faced that come close. Humbaba, one of the bosses you have to face to get the esper Chaos only had a little more than 300k, and that was the most of anything I had faced so far. To kick it off, find Rikken in **Balfonheim Port / Saccio Lane**, just in front of the entrance to Reddas's place (I wish Balfonheim had a moogling like Rabanastre). You may have to talk to him a second time as he first might say something related to the storyline. He may also challenge you to a footrace first and only tell you about the bill afterward.

He also won't give you any clues as to where to find the mark. I couldn't find anybody else giving clues, either. So you can explore the whole world until you stumble across it, or you can teleport to **Pharos** and walks outside into **They Who Thirst Not** and get toseed into the fray.

> ##### Hunt: Pylraster
>
>
> | **Level** | 63 | **LP** | 36 | **CP** | 12050 |
> | **HP** | 493513 | **Steal** | Tyrant Hide (55/80%), Tyrant Bone (10/30%), Hastega Mote (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Fire |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Water<br>\[Half Damage\] Ice, Lightning, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Slow, Libra |
>
> So, the easiest way to win this is to keep Pylraster's focus on a tank that has Reverse status on. This will cause Pylraster's attacks to heal rather than damage. Be sure to turn of any Esuna gambits, however, as they won't be needed and they will target allies that have Reverse status. Also, if Pylraster stops targetting your tank, get rid of Reverse as your Curaja gambit will cause a lot of damage and trigger Curaja again until the character dies.
>
> It's helpful to have a gun user with Wyrmfire Shot since guns ignore defense. A black-magick user with Faith status casting Firaga with a Flame Staff equipped can also do major damage (mine was doing between 30-35k). Bravery on your tank is also helpful at getting through its half million HP. Keep up Protect, Haste, and Bubble on everyone. If you can't do all of this with one party, you can forego the Reverse idea and make sure that your tank has very high evasion (equip a shield...). This is one of the few bosses that never gets the Ignore Evade hidden status. The boosted Firaga made this battle quite quick in spite of the massive HP. If you found it disappointing, maybe the next will be tough enough for you.

| **Hunt Completed** | Pylraster |
| **Rewards** | 8000 Gil<br>Grand Mace (x1)<br>Scathe Mote (x1) |

Head back to Rikken in **Balfonheim Port / Saccio Lane** to collect your reward. Again, you may have to speak to him twice. At least he won't challenge you to a foot race again. If, for some odd reason, you actually enjoyed the foot race and it reminds you of the good ole days with Hippaul (FF IX), by all means talk to him again and he will be glad to race you. Every time you win you get a new prize up to the 100th time. Almost none of the prizes are worth the time the race takes. Most are items available from the shops. Every 10th tier gets you something nicer, usually motes, and the 100th gets you a couple of Elixirs, an Unpurified Ether, and 10000 gil. If you're a completionist, go for it. Otherwise, don't waste your time.

#### Wyrm Wrath's Renewal

While the bill says you need to find Ieena, instead speak with the Viera Relj in **Mt Bur-Omisace / Temple Aproach**.

Fafnir only comes out in a blizzard. Fortunately, there will be a blizzard throught **Paramina Rift** as long as you don't enter **Frozen Brook**. So to reach Fafnir, you need to exit **Mt Bur-Omisace** and then take the west exit out of **Freezing Gorge**. Take the south exit in **Head of the Silverflow** and the southwestern exit in **Icebound Flow**. This will put you in **Karydine Glacier** just north of the entrance to **Silverflow's End** where you will find your mark.

> ##### Hunt: Fafnir
>
>
> | **Level** | 68 | **LP** | 39 | **CP** | 18620 |
> | **HP** | 1390378 | **Steal** | Pebble (55/80%), Hi-Potion (10/30%), Ring Wyrm Scale (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Lightning |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Ice<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Sap, Slow |
>
> The toughest part of this battle (other than the amount of damage this beast can deal) is the status ailments. Fafnir uses White Wind, which can cause Stop, and likes to cast Silencega and Sleepga. Your primary damage dealer should probably be a black magick user, so it's best to equip an accessory that prevents one of these (Rose Corsage for Silence, Nishijin Belt for Sleep, and Power Armlet for Stop). You may want to do the same for your healer. Set up gambits to help with the other statuses.
>
> Set up your black magick user with a staff that boosts thunder magick (Cloud Staff or Staff of the Magi). Have that character cast Thundaga the whole battle while under Faith status. It should deal over 20000 damage per shot. Keep Protect, Shell, Bubble, and Regen on as well - and Haste, if you can manage.
>
> Start the battle with Dispel so that your Thundagas will get through. The first half of this battle is primarily managing status ailments and keeping your healer's MP up. Use Hi-Ethers so that you don't waste so much time using Charge after every cast. Be sure your healer has Curaja and Arise gambits set up. It's better to use Curaja early than have to raise a KOed party member because if someone is KOed they lose all their beneficial statuses.
>
> The second half of the battle you get to deal with Fafnirs devastating magick attacks: Scourge and Shock. If you are really quick, as soon as you see Fafnir ready the spell equip the target with Mirror Mail. This will reflect the spell and doe several thousand damage. Be sure to swap your armor back immediately so you don't end up reflecting a Curaja.
>
> As Fafnir hits harder and faster the further the battle goes, I did end up with a few KOs for party members with my party level at 57. This was probably doable even at levels closer to 50.

| **Hunt Completed** | Fafnir |
| **Rewards** | 7000 Gil<br>Assassin's Arrows (x1)<br>Teleport Stone (x1) |

#### The Child Snatcher

To find the petitioner for Diabolos, head to **Bhujerba / Miner's End** and look for Miclio, a young child standing behind a fence near the southern corner.

Make your way into **Lhusu Mines** and work toward **Site 11**. Diabolos should appear in the southwestern chamber.

> ##### Hunt: Diabolos
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 10 | **CP** | 3800 |
> | **HP** | 93551 | **Steal** | Fire Crystal (55/80%), Demon's Sigh (10/30%), Scorpio Gem (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Water |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Fire<br>\[Half Damage\] Ice, Lightning, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Slow |
>
> While Diabolos is weak to Water, Aqua doesn't do much damage. Your best option is Aqua Shot and Genji-ed up physical attackers. The most dangerous attack early on is the spell Curse, followed by Scourge. Be ready to remove all the bad statuses these inflict. Don't bother with Espers as Diabolos is able to wipe them out quickly with Breakart Pentagram.
>
> Overall, if you have done these marks in sequence, you should be able to knock out Diabolos's 93551 HP incredibly quickly after Fafnir's million plus. This prevents Diabolos from messing with your party with status ailments.

| **Hunt Completed** | Diabolos |
| **Rewards** | 2600 Gil<br>Demon Shield (x1)<br>Zeus Mace (x1) |

This may be a good time to go deeper into the mines to face Disma, if you are doing the Hunt Club. Equip Black Masks, Black Robes, and Rubber Suits to protect against the main attacks and be prepared for a tough fight. Otherwise, head back to Miclio who is still in the same spot in **Bhujerba / Miner's End**. Congratulations on finishing all of the regular hunts! Only four more elite marks to go. After you're conversation with Miclio, speak to the Miners' Ender right next to you for some amusing dialogue.

### Optional Area - Subterra and Ixion, Shadowseer, Behemoth King

If you've been following the guide, the only areas left to explore are **Pharos Subterra**, a major chunk of **The Great Crystal**, and a portion of **Henne Mines**. Each one has elite marks, espers, optional bosses, rare game, magick, and technicks that all need to be picked up. Since there is generally some combination of each, it's probably best to cover them section by section rather than quest by quest.

I'm going to start with **Pharos Subterra** as it doesn't have any of the superbosses. Before coming here, make sure that you have accepted the hunts for Ixion and the Shadowseer (or The Seer). You probably got Shadowseer automatically from Montblanc, but still need to grab the hunt for Ixion from its petitioner. Head to **Balfonheim Port / Quayside Court** and go into the tavern **The Whitecap**. To the right of the entrance, behind the counter on the highest level, the Whitecap Wench is waiting to hire you.

#### Pharos Subterra

To reach **Pharos Subterra**, teleport to **Pharos** and look for the Dais of Ascendance on the first floor. It gives you the option of traveling to **Penumbra** now.

First, know that Ixion can be faced on any **Interior** section in **Subterra**. As such, it may be best to mention the strategy against the mark right now.

#### Fishy Dreams

If you are struggling to get Ixion to appear, it only appears when you come directly from the 1st Floor and no other enemies will be present if it is going to spawn. Keep taking the lift between the 1st floor and alternate the others until you do not see any foes. Ixion may not be present immediately but will appear in less than three and a half minutes (usually faster).

> ##### Hunt: Ixion
>
>
> | **Level** | 58 | **LP** | 32 | **CP** | 5000 |
> | **HP** | 306559 | **Steal** | Storm Crystal (55/80%), Grimoire Aidhed (10/30%), Magick Lamp (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Dark<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Silence, Oil, Sap, Slow, Berserk |
>
> Ixion uses almost every -ga spell in the game. All of the elemental versions plus Silencega, Sleepga, Fearga, and Dispelga. So, it's lack of immunity to Silence can be taken advantage of by using something like Silent Shot with a gun user. It can remove Silence with Purify, but it still buys you some time. Additionally, it is susceptible to Berserk. You can apply that with magick or with Bacchus's Wine.If you cast Berserk, you may also want to keep it Slowed and try to keep your party members buffed with Protect, Bubble, and Bravery for your melee fighters. You can also cast Bio to inflict Sap, but keep in mind that Ixion will remove the status.
>
> Be sure to start the battle with Dispel as Ixion starts with quite a few buffs. If you've already completed most of **Pharos** **\- Subterra**, you should have a Ribbon. Make sure someone is equipped with it to prevent your whole party from being silenced or put to sleep simultaneously. Once Ixion's HP drops below 50%, casting Bio will cause Ixion to put up a magick shield preventing all magick damage. At that point, either swap gambits so your caster will start attacking or swap party members for another physical fighter. If you have a weapon that causes Sap (Stink Bombs, Chopper, Blood Sword), it might be worth equipping since forcing Ixion to use Purify often is extremely beneficial.

| **Hunt Completed** | Ixion |
| **Rewards** | 3000 Gil<br>Sapping Bolts (x1)<br>Ragnarok (x1) |

Whenever is convenient, head back to the Whitecap Wench for your reward. If you didn't have a good weapon to cause Sap, you now have some Crossbow Bolts that do.

#### Pharos Subterra / Penumbra

| **Treasures** | 5 ( **Penumbra - North**), 3 ( **Penumbra South**) |
| **Enemies** | Abaddon, Bull Abaddon, Mistmare, Bune, Nightwalker, Necrofiend, Magick Pot, Dragon Lich, Reaver, High Reaver, Phoenix (boss) |

You come off the Dais of Ascendance on the west side of the ring. Turn north and get to the first Ancient Door.

It will be very dark when you enter. Ascend the platform in front of you and turn left. Open the Ancient Door and begin fighting enemies to gather Black Orbs. If you turn north in the first room, there should be a massive Black Orb in the next that will give you three Black Orbs. Return to the room where you entered the section and look in the northwest corner for a Pedestal to place your Black Orbs. Once you place 9, the whole northwest area will brighten. Make your way to the northeast section of **Penumbra - North** by returning to **Penumra - Interior**.

You will have the same goal of collecting Black Orbs, but it will take 18 this time. There is only one door out of the entrance chamber and it is on the east side. There is a Fool's Facade on the north wall of the first secret room. There is an exit to the west and an Ancient Door to the east of the next room. The next room has a chamber to the north and an Ancient Door to the west, behind which is a boss that must be defeated before certain rare monsters will appear and before you can face Shadowseer.

> ##### Boss: Phoenix
>
>
> | **Level** | 59 | **LP** | 28 | **CP** | 5220 |
> | **HP** | 134089 | **Steal** | Windslicer Pinion (55/80%), Eye of the Hawk (10/30%), Split Armor (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Varies |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | Varies |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Oil, Libra |
>
> The best strategy here is to set up gambits for a Black Mage to target each individual elemental weakness with the corresponding -ga spell. Keep Faith on your Black Mage and equip a staff that boosts multiple elements (like Cloud Staff or Staff of the Magi). Phoenix will consistently change which element it is weak to and will absorb all of the others. By having gambits set this way, your Black Mage will automatically select the best one. Ranged weapons that are non-elemental are also necessary since Phoenix is flying. A Tula Crossbow with Black Bolts can be helpful since Phoenix is not immune to Blind. Other than that, be sure to have good healing gambits set up and good buffs (Bubble, Protect, Haste, Regen - Shell isn't necessary). If your Black Mage isn't casting anything, try Flare. The battle was not difficult at all at level 61 with these gambits in place.

You can find a door on the west side of Phoenix's chamber. If you encounter a Magick Pot, be sure to turn all gambits off and either avoid it or give it an Elixir before turning gambits back on. It will not attack after it has been given an Elixir, but it can be extremely challenging if provoked without giving it an Elixir. Continue fighting in **Penumbra - North** until you have the 18 Black Orbs to light up the northeast pedestal. You can always drop off what you have and it will still brighten the area proportionally, but in order to proceed to the next level all pedestals will need to be filled. This may require you to grab some from **Penumbra - South**. Head back into **Penumbra - Interior** and continue clockwise until you reach the next door.

You will only need 3 Black Orbs for the southeast pedestal, but there is only one off-map room. It is to the east of the entrance chamber. In the chamber I found a Dragon Lich guarding a Massive Black Orb that yielded 12 Black Orbs. That was enough for the Southeast Pedestal and finish the Northeast.

There is one more pedestal in the southwest chamber, requiring 6 Black Orbs. There are two room off this chamber, west and south. Once you have enough Black Orbs, fill the remaining pedestals to enable travel down to the **Umbra** level. You may want to stop and save on the 1st floor first.

#### Pharos Subterra / Umbra

| **Treasures** | 4 ( **Umbra - North**), 3 ( **Penumbra South**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Nihopalaoa |
| **Enemies** | Mistmare, Dead Bones, Necrofiend Nightwalker, Dragon Lich, Magick Pot, Luxollid (rare game) |

You will have a similar task on this level as the previous: fill the four pedestals with sufficient Black Orbs. The layout is similar, but not identical. The large central chamber in **Umbra - South** has a 5% chance of spawning a one-time Nihopalaoa in the southwest corener in the tiny alcove behind the further Dragon Lich. Definitely get this if you don't already have one. It can take quite a while, though. I probably required about 50 or so re-entries, but pocketed about half a dozen Elixirs from the center chest in the process. This accessory allows you to inflict statuses on foes by using the item that would cure the status. Be careful, though, it does the same to you.This is also the room where the rare game Luxollid can spawn, but only after defeating Vagrant Soul in the **Abyssal** level.

Rather than try to walk you through all the different chambers like before, here is a table summarizing what you need to know:

| Pedestal | Black Orbs required | side chambers | Massive Black Orbs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Northwest | 9 | west (3 rooms), north (1 room) |  |
| Northeast | 15 | east (2 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room) | In first room |
| Southeast | 15 | east (5 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room) |  |
| Southwest | 18 | west (1 room), south (4 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room) | Corner of L-shaped room |

If you had any Black Orbs from **Penumbra**, they will come with you and can be used to get things started here in **Umbra**. Once you've filled all the pedestals, the Dais will take you to the lowest level **Abyssal**.

#### Pharos Subterra - Abyssal

| **Treasures** | 9 ( **Abyssal - North**), 9 ( **Abyssal South**) |
| **Notable Loot** | Ribbon, Wither (technick), Megalixir, Great Trango |
| **Enemies** | Necrofiend, Dead Bones, Zombie Warlock, Magick Pot, Nightwalker, Dragon Lich, Vagrant Soul (rare game) |

You will again need to place sufficient Black Orbs on four pedestal in order to light up each quadrant and give access to the lowest level of **Subterra**.

| Pedestal | Black Orbs required | side chambers | Massive Black Orbs |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Northwest | 21 | west (3 rooms), north (4 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room) | Corner of L-shaped room in the north group |
| Northeast | 15 | east (4 rooms, Fool's Facade in 2nd room) | Third room, not behind Fool's Facade |
| Southeast | 27 | east (7 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room and 2nd room) |  |
| Southwest | 12 | west (3 rooms, Fool's Facade in 1st room), south (1 room) | First room west |

Up until now, almost every treasure you've encountered could have had gear - but it was always stuff you could have gotten somewhere else more easily. On this level, there are a few treasures absolutely worth spawning. In the northwest quadrant, in the second room to the west there is a one-time Ribbon that spawns 10% of the time. This is definitely worth grabbing. In the same room is a chest that spawns more often that can hold Quasimodo Boots or a Scathe Mote when it doesn't hold 1-3 gil. In the room north of that (accessible from the first west room), there is a chest that can have an Opal Ring or Icecloud Arrows, but holds 1-3 gil 80% of the time. Even so, it might be worth checking in here every time you try to spawn the Ribbon. If you go north from the entry chamber, the first room may have a chest that will sometimes hold Hermes Sandals or an Elixir. The next room's treasure can hold a Bubble Belt or Dark Shot. Two rooms to the west is where the rare game Vagrant Soul will spawn after the Shadowseer has been defeated. In that room, there is a 10% chance for the technick Wither to spawn in the center of the room.

In the northeast quadrant, there are three treasures that can spawn, on in each of the last three rooms. They can hold Jackboots, a Sash, a Ruby Ring, a Scathe Mote, a Holy Mote, or Dark Matter. Most often they'll hold 3 gil or less.

In the southern quadrants, always equip a Diamond Armlet before opening the treasures. They will only hold Knots of Rust without the Diamond Armlet, but they may hold a Baltoro's seed with it equipped. In the large central chamber accessible from the southeast quadrant, there is a one-time guaranteed Megalixir and a 2% chance for a chest that has an extremely low likelihood of holding the Great Trango sword when it does appear. If you really want this, it may be best to do the RNG manipulation, otherwise you may be trying for years before you get it.

Once all the pedestals have been filled, be sure to head back to the first floor to save before descending to the lowest level, **Hell's Challenge**, labelled as Unknown on the Dais of Ascendance.

#### God or Devil?

As soon as you arrive at the lowest level, you will be thrown into battle.

> ##### Hunt: Shadowseer
>
>
> | **Level** | 67 | **LP** | 6 | **CP** | 5590 |
> | **HP** | 278078 | **Steal** | Foul Flesh (55/80%), Maggoty Flesh (10/30%), Forbidden Flesh (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Dark<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Oil, Libra |
>
> After Shadowseer loses 20% of its health, it will maintain a paling preventing all damage until you defeat four bosses you have previously encountered: Pandaemonium, Slyt, Fenrir, and Phoenix. The only difference between the previous fights and these is that you have Shadowseer disrupting as well, frequently using Curse which causes Disease and a variety of status ailments. The most annoying of these can be Confuse, so be sure to have some Remedy gambits ready. Shadowseer often uses Fearga, as well, damaging your MP substantially. Because Fearga gets used so often, it's best to have a Charge gambit instead of a Hi-Ether.
>
> It's important to maintain Protect, Shell, Bubble, and if possible Regen and Haste. Shadowseer will periodically use Dispelga, possibly removing the buffs, but keeping them up is key to survival. Make sure you have your black magick user with gambits set up for each weakness for the fight against Phoenix, as before. If your main damage dealer is not a magick user, be sure to have a gambit that targets flying foes with something like Gil Toss (assuming you can afford to throw away a few hundred thousand gil).
>
> Once Phoenix is wiped out, Shadowseer is a little easier to deal with. It will be able to take damage again, but it will use high level magick like Scourge, Scathe, Shock, Flare, and Holy. All of these can deal several thousand damage. Expect your healer to be casting Curaja often. It's best to have it set up so that it will cast if anyone is less than 6000 HP, whatever percentage of your HP that corresponds to.
>
> For set up, I had my Knight / Bushi with the Khumba and full Genji Gear. My Black Mage / Monk used a Cloud Staff until Phoenix was finished (I switched to Whale Whisker afterward), with the Golden Skullcap and Glimmering Robes. My White Mage / Machinist was using the Arcturus and Silent Shot ammo, a Rubber Suit, the Renewing Morion, and a Bowline Sash accessory to prevent Confusion. My party level was 65. There was one point where I had two characters KOed, but that was the only point that felt perilous.

| **Hunt Completed** | Shadowseer |
| **Rewards** | 20000 Gil<br>Megalixir (x2) |

After the fight, you will be placed back on the 1st floor. Use the Save Crystal to replenish health and MP. Now is a good time to head down to **Abyssal** to fight Vagrant Soul (be sure to steal the Shikari Nagasa) and then up to **Umbra** to fight Luxollid (from which you can steal the Sagittarius). After that, as long as You've already encountered and faced Ixion, you should be completely done with **Pharos - Subterra**. Now you can return to Montblanc to grab your reward and pick up the next mark. While here in **Rabanastre**, head to Old Dalan's Place in **Lowtown - South** to find the petitioner Koqmihn.

#### Truth Shrouded in Mist

Behemoth King is no joke. Expect to take damage even from hitting it. As such, I went and got everyone's level above 70 by farming Dark Lord in **Nabreau Deadlands / Overlooking Eternity**. With the x4 speed on and Embroidered Tippets, you can gain levels fairly quickly. Once you know where the chain breakers are in **The Fog Mutters**, you can time when to flee past to reset **Overlooking Eternity** without breaking your chain. Once you get the highest level chain, you'll be rolling in drops and the drops will keep you healed and MP filled, so you don't even have to worry about casting buffs. Once you feel like you've leveled enough, teleport to **Giruvegan**.

Backtrack into **The Feywood / Edge of Reason**. Find and kill the Giruveganus and head northeast to **Ice Field of Clearsight**. Kill every enemy here (should be 29), then return to where the Giruveganus was. If it is back, kill it again and head back to **Ice Field of Clearsight** and look for any missed enemies. If you get the Mandragoras, be sure to kill all 5 and return to **Edge of Reason**. If it wasn't there before, Behemoth King should definitely now be roaming where the Giruveganus used to be.

> ##### Hunt: Behemoth King
>
>
> | **Level** | 70 | **LP** | 40 | **CP** | 19530 |
> | **HP** | 1668491 | **Steal** | Ring Wyrm Scale (55/80%), Behemoth Steak (10/30%), Elixir (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> Go in with one character carrying Genji Gear and a really good sword (Great Trango or Khumba). That character needs to have Reverse and Decoy maintained the whole battle, but expect to have to resurrect the character often. Also try to keep up Bravery, Bubble, and Haste (in that order). Turn off all healing gambits - you'll have to handle this manually. Regen, Protect, and Shell are good on your support characters, but not on your Reverse character - so they need individual target gambits for those or you will need to do them manually. Most likely, you won't have time to maintain those. You'll be too busy keeping the important statuses on your Reverse character. Every character (including your Reverse character) needs a Ally:any Chrono's Tear gambit and Phoenix Down or Arise gambit. One person should also have a Foe:any Dispel gambit.
>
> Behemoth King may start alternating being immune to physical (Magick Wall) and magickal damage (Anti-Magick). It's possible that it will never erect these barriers. If it becomes immune to physical, however, you should cast Arise twice to cause it to shift its immunity.
>
> The difficulty in this battle is staying alive. Manually use Elixirs on your support characters when needed, but let Reverse handle healing on your damage dealer. Try to keep your characters spread out around Behemoth King so that they can't all be affected by multi-target spells. If you have two characters down, get one up and try to use an Elixir. Run around, forcing Behemoth King to follow you and get in fewer attacks while you get everyone back on their feet. If you have to completely run away for a little bit, do so but don't take long.

| **Hunt Completed** | Shadowseer |
| **Rewards** | 250 Gil<br>Bacchus's Wine (x2) |
| **Secondary Rewards** | 500000 Gil<br>Rod of Faith (x1) |

At this point, I would recommend obtaining any espers you may have missed. By this point, I had obtained all except [Ultima](#lists-for-trophies) and [Zodiark](#lists-for-trophies). Obtaining these two will take you to the last areas left to explore as well. If you've been following this guide so far, in addition to the trophies for mastering the individual espers, you should also get the following trophies along the way:

### The Superbosses - Hell Wyrm, Yiazmat, Omega Mark XII

There are two optional bosses known as "superbosses" to face. One is the final Clan Centurio hunt, Yiazmat. The other is a giant mimic in **The Great Crystal**, Omega Mark XII. Omega Mark XII will remain dormant until you have accepted the hunt for Yiazmat. The hunt for Yiazmat won't be available until you defeat an optional high-level boss called Hell Wyrm. You can't access Hell Wyrm without the Dragon Scale you get from the hunt [Get My Stuff Back.](#get-my-stuff-back)

#### Go to Hell Wyrm

> ##### Boss: Hell Wyrm
>
>
> | **Level** | 60 | **LP** | 150 | **CP** | 30000 |
> | **HP** | 8930711 | **Steal** | Dark Stone (55/80%), Dark Magicite (10/30%), Dark Crystal(3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Dark<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Oil, Berserk |
>
> Hell Wyrm is weak to Holy, but also uses a skill called Judgment that does Holy damage every time its HP drops 10%. It also uses every elemental -ga spell. Its most common attacks are Rake and Stone Breath, but once it reaches 10% HP it will start using Invert very frequently (about every other command). This means that the first 90% of the battle can use one gambit setup, and the last 10% needs one small tweak. With the following setup, the fight is almost automatic, even if lengthy.
>
> |  | Attacker: Knight/Bushi |  | Healer/Buffer: White Mage |  | Backup Healer/Status Recovery: Black Mage/Monk |
> | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
> | **Weapon** | Excalibur |  | Holy Rod |  | Staff of the Magi |
> | **Off-hand** |  |  |  |  |  |
> | **Helm** | Genji Helm |  | Golden Skullcap |  | White Mask |
> | **Armor** | Genji Armor |  | Black Robes |  | Lordly Robes |
> | **Accessory** | Genji Gloves |  | Sage's Ring |  | Ribbon |
> | **Slot** | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |
> | 1 | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |  | Ally: HP <60% | Curaja |  | Ally: any | Chronos Tear |
> | 2 | Ally: any | Remedy |  | Ally: any | Arise |  | Ally: HP < 40% | X-Potion |
> | 3 | Ally: HP < 30% | X-Potion |  | Ally: any | Esuna |  | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |
> | 4 | Foe: lowest HP | Attack |  | Ally: _Attacker_ | Bravey |  | Ally: any | Bubble |
> | 5 |  |  |  | Self | Faith |  | Ally: Any | Remedy |
> | 6 |  |  |  | Ally: Black Mage | Faith |  | Foe: lowest HP | Scourge |
> | 7 |  |  |  | Ally: any | Regen |  | Self: MP < 10% | Hi-Ether |
> | 8 |  |  |  | Ally: any | Protectga |  |  |  |
> | 9 |  |  |  | Ally: any | Shellga |  |  |  |
> | 10 |  |  |  | Ally: any | Bubble |  |  |  |
> | 11 |  |  |  | Self: MP < 10% | Hi-Ether |  |  |  |
> | 12 |  |  |  | Foe: lowest HP | Holy |  |  |  |
>
> The main idea is to have your main magick users equipped to absorb Holy damage to get a break periodically. Since your healer does most of the casting, the Sage's Ring helps with MP management. You want two casters using Bubble, as Hell Wyrm can have some pretty high combos and attacks fairly quickly. Its also really big and your party can get spread pretty far apart. If you notice a gambit not activating, manually switch party members and maneuver everyone back close together again - but not so close they all get hit with Stone Breath. The rest of the gear is to maximize Holy damage dealt or Scourge damage dealt. Cronos Tear comes at the top of the gambit list of the person with a Ribbon because Judgement has a very high probability of inflicting Stop. Even though Shell mitigates that somewhat, I frequently found myself with only that one character available. You don't want to waste time trying to throw X-Potions solo. Obviously, Faith and Bravery help speed things up, and are very important so that the healer can restore all HP in one shot of Curaja. Bravery is more important than Protect and Shell especially toward the end of the fight when Invert happens repeatedly. Your best defense, at that point, is a better offense. Once Hell Wyrm gets down below 10%, turn of the X-Potion gambit on your attacker. Invert happens so often that most of the attacks will get interrupted by trying to toss and X-Potion. One person using X-Potions and another casting Curaja is enough generally to respond to every Invert, but those two characters will no longer be doing damage. You need to have at least one person focused on finishing the fight.
>
> There are other strategies to the fight, such as inflicting Oil and using all fire-based magick and weapons, or using Wither, Addle, Expose, and Shear repeatedly to make Hell Wyrm a bit easier. I don't see how either really helps with the only tough portion of the fight (when HP is less than 10%) or is an improvement over taking advantage of the existing weakness to Holy damage since the Holy-based weapons are already some of the most powerful. There's nothing wrong with combining the approaches or using one of the others if you don't already have the gear. Because I saved this sequence for last, my levels were already a bit higher: 73 for the Knight / Bushi, 75 for the White Mage, and 78 for the Black Mage / Monk. Other than how long it took, I don't think being several levels lower would have made much difference.

After the fight, grab the treasures in the room. There are three guaranteed one-time treasures and one one-time treasure with a 1% appearance rate. The guaranteed treasures include a Reverse Mote, a Hastega Mote, and a Bubble Mote. The rare treasure is the Zodiac Escutcheon. Now that Hell Wyrm is gone, you can rezone repeatedly to get it, if you have the patience. It should spawn on the north wall just to the right of center. Afterwards, go tell the person who always rewards great deeds: Montblanc. If you've already defeated all the Espers, he may have some other rewards for you too. For defeating Hell Wyrm, _50000 gil_. For commanding 13 Espers, _Serpentarius_ and _Teleport Stones (x2)_. You can now ask about the very last mark. Accept the hunt and the two superbosses will be available.

#### Farewell to a Legend

In preparation for the next fight, make sure you have a full stock of Phoenix Downs, Chronos Tears, Remedies, and Bacchus's Wine. For reference, I used 34 Phoenix Downs, 25 Chronos Tears, 39 Remedies, 59 Bacchus's Wine, and 4 Hi-Ethers. Also, if you haven't gotten a [Yagyu Darkblade](#yagyu-darkblade) and a [Kumbha](#kumbha), I strongly recommend it just to cut down the length of time. Similarly, if you got the second Genji Glove by doing the [Hunt Club](#optional---endgame-sidequests), that will help speed things along. Finally, the technicks Wither, Addle, Shear, and Expose are essential for surviving this battle. With all of that in order, I did this battle with all of my primary active party at level 75 or below, and only using one of my inactive party to periodically resurrect and another to cast debuffs and trigger traps at the beginning. If you have a level 75 party with all of the gear and items listed above, then you should be ready.

Teleport to **Pharos at Ridorana** and head back outside into the **Ridorana Cataract**. When you reach **City of Other Days**, turn south and head to the **Colosseum**. For convenience sake, set up your characters before you enter the **Colosseum** so that you have an autosave file with everything in place. If you follow this guide, however, you shouldn't ever be in any serious danger. And if you are, either you got very unlucky or you were not paying attention. Other than a few gambits that need to be toggled as planned, I only had to intervene about five or six times to bring my healer back to life. Here is your initial set up for the four main characters you will need:

|  | Primary Attacker: Knight/Bushi |  | Debuffer & Attacker: Foebreaker/Shikari |  | Secondary Debuffer: Monk/Black Mage |  | Healer/Buffer: White Mage/Machinist |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Weapom** | Kumbha |  | Yagyu Darkblade |  | Staff of the Magi |  | Formalhaut |
| **Off-hand** | Shell Shield |  |  |  |  |  | Dark Shot |
| **Helm** | Golden Skullcap |  | Renewing Morion |  | White Mask |  | Golden Skullcap |
| **Armor** | Black Robes |  | Maximilian |  | Lordly Robes |  | Black Robes |
| **Accessory** | Genji Gloves |  | Genji Gloves |  | Bubble Belt |  | Ribbon |
| **Slot** | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |  | **Condition** | **Action** |
| 1 | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |  | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |  | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |  | Ally: any | Phoenix Down |
| 2 | Ally: any | Chronos Tear |  | Ally: any | Chronos Tear |  | Ally: any | Chronos Tear |  | Ally: any | Chronos Tear |
| 3 | Ally: any | Remedy |  | Ally: any | Remedy |  | Ally: any | Remedy |  | Ally: any | Remedy |
| 4 | Self | Bacchus's Wine |  | Foe: any | Addle |  | Foe: any | Wither |  | Ally: status = Reflect | Dispelga |
| 5 |  |  |  | Foe: any | Shear |  | Foe: any | Expose |  | Ally: HP < 40% | Curaja |
| 6 |  |  |  | Self | Bacchus's Wine |  |  |  |  | Ally: _Attacker_ | Bravery |
| 7 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Ally: _Other attacker_ | Bravery |
| 8 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Ally: _Debuffer & Attacker_ | Decoy |
| 9 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Ally: any | Protectga |
| 10 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Ally: any | Shellga |
| 11 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Self: MP < 20% | Hi-Ether |
| 12 |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  |  | Foe: any | Attack |

Before leaving the menus, make sure your active party does not include the Primary Attacker for now. On your other two characters, the only gambits they need are Phoenix Downs, and both should be equipped with Light Armor and Bubble Belts so that they have a lot of HP if they get swapped in. Another thing to note, do not have the Shellga gambit turned on yet. Only turn it on if you see Yiazmat starting to use Death Strike more often once its HP is below 50%. One last thing: go to Config and set your battle speed to Fastest. Yiazmat has no charge time on its attacks, so the faster you can execute your commands the better. Yiazmat will not speed up, only your characters.

Alright, now that you are set up, head into the **Colosseum**. You will see Yiazmat in front of you. There are strategies that involve fighting it from the hallways where it cannot reach you, and that's what I used on the PS2 version years ago. That strategy took me well over three hours back then. This strategy got me through in under twenty minutes (I did have the x4 speed turned on). Approach Yiazmat to start the battle.

> ##### Hunt: Yiazmat
>
>
> | **Level** | 73 | **LP** | 255 | **CP** | 20510 |
> | **HP** | 50112254 | **Steal** | Holy Stone (55/80%), Holy Magicite (10/30%), Bone of Byblos (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Dark |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Holy<br>\[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> Yiazmat's attacks all have a chance to cause instant KO. As such, expect to be using a lot of Phoenix Downs. It also uses White Breath causing Stop, Stone Breath causing Petrify, and Cyclone causing Sap. Thus the need for the top priority gambits being Phoenix Down, Chronos Tear, and Remedy. It's a good idea to have those even on the two characters you won't be using much, just in case they are needed for resurrecting. Later in the battle, Yiazmat will start using Death Strike. It has a 100% chance to KO unless the target has Shell status. Since Yiazmat may rarely use it before it has low HP, it's best to have a Shell Shield equipped on the person targeted by Decoy. This drops the KO rate to 50%, and every bit helps.
>
> When the battle starts, your first objectiv is to decrease Yiazmat's ability to do damage. Your two debuffers should be spamming Addle and Whither. These can stack multiple times, and you will get a message every time it is successful. It gets harder to stack the more stacks already present, but eventually cannot stack at all. At this point, Yiazmat's damage output will be so low that the only concern you'll have is the status effects and how long the battle will take. We can't stop the status effects, but we can decrease the length of the battle.
>
> If you have not received the message that Addle and Whither have connected for a while and you keep seeing _Miss_, go into your gambits and turn those off. This will cause that character to switch to debuffing Yiazmat's defenses. Repeat this until you aren't seeing any more successful Shear or Expose attempts. While this is going on, have your secondary debuffer as the party leader and run around triggering all the traps that are around Yiazmat. The trap Yiazmat is standing on is an instant death trap. If you can't get to it, that's probably okay. Yiazmat stayed over it the entire battle for me, so it wasn't an issue. If Yiazmat moves, be sure to get it triggered during this part of the battle if possible.
>
> Once the traps are triggered and you've stacked as much of the four debuffs as possible, turn off the debuff gambits for both characters and swap your secondary debuffer out for your primary attacker. Also, change the Decoy gambit to target the new attacker wearing the Shell Shield. If you don't have a Shell Shield, it doesn't matter who Decoy targets as long as it isn't targeting the healer! At this point, both attackers should start using Bacchus's Wine and Yiazmat's HP will start dropping very quickly. Yagyu Darkblade and Kumbha both combo well. Genji Gloves assist with that. Yagyu Darkblade and Dark Shot also take advantage of Yiazmat's weakness to Dark damage. While the first part of the battle probably did only around a half of a single bar, you should now start going through a bar every five to ten seconds. My attackers were doing 20-25k damage per hit at this point.
>
> While this is going on, have your healer as the party leader and try to stay out of range of Yiazmat's Rake attack as it has high combo rates and the chance to KO. If your healer manages to be in the way, that character will likely get KOed. Whenever your healer gets KOed, immediately swap out whichever attacker isn't being targeted with one of your final two characters. As soon as you see the Healer get resurrected wth a Phoenix Down, swap the attacker back in. The gambits are set up so that you should get recovered fairly quickly without much chance of a follow-up KO. Just switch party leader to the healer and move that character out of the way again. If you see a status not being treated, make sure your healer is close enough to trigger the gambit. Having the ranged weapon on your healer will cause that character to continusously back up. That's good to avoid Rake, but can cause the gambits to fail so periodically scoot back in.
>
> Once Yiazmat's HP drops sufficiently, it will start using the skill Death Strike. It will use this all battle if you only have ranged fighters, but once its HP drops it will use it on anyone. At that point, turn on the Shellga gambit for your healer. The Shell Shield protected the primary attacker against the rare cast in the first half of the battle, but now you are more likely going to see multiple characters get targeted.
>
> The last trick Yiazmat has up its sleeve is that it will cast Reflectga on the party somewhere around the 20% HP mark. This is the reason for the Dispelga gambit. It only happens once, but you don't want to be in the middle of a Curaja cast when Reflect gets applied. By having that gambit above all other magick casting, your healer will cancel any other magick the moment Reflect is detected. You'll have to reapply all of your status buffs again, but that really is only a few seconds delay. There isn't really much danger.
>
> If you played the originial PS2 release and wondered why this strategy wasn't available then - it was. It just wasn't as useful. The damage cap was 9999, so doing all the debuffs didn't really make a difference because a berserked character with a Yagyu Darkblade was already doing 9999 damage. When you are doing 2.5 times that damage AND you have the speed multiplier, the entire battle time is cut to one-tenth what it was in the PS2 version. Given that it took me about 20 minutes, that times ten would be 200 minutes. It took me about 3.5 hours on the PS2 - 210 minutes.

| **Hunt Completed** | Yiazmat |
| **Rewards** | 30000 Gil<br>Godslayer's Badge (x1) |

#### Saving the Best for Omega

Omega Mark XII is deep in **The Great Crystal**. If you haven't faced [Ultima](#lists-for-trophies) yet, you will need to at least get through that portion of the walkthrough up to the fight with Ultima. If you have faced Ultima and followed the walkthrough to get the treasures, then your job is much easier as all the correct Way Stones will be active and the correct gates opened. So, if you haven't faced Ultima, visit that portion of the walkthrough to get step-by-step instructions as it is quite complicated. If you have faced Ultima, teleport to the Giruvegan Crystal and make you way into **The Great Crystal**. Head up to Way Stone IX like you did to get to Ultima, then use it to teleport to Way Stone X. Leave that zone and use Way Stone XX to teleport to Way Stone IX. Leave the zone and you will be at the Save Crystal outside of Ultima's chamber. There are four exits from this zone. Use the one not adjacent to any other exits to find Way Stone XVIII and use it to reach Way Stone XVII. Leave this zone and angle to the right in the next. In the following zone take a sharp left to reach the Libra Gate Stone. Angle left to reach a zone with a Skulwyrm. Of the two exits, only one is available off to the right. Go straight through the next zone. If there is a gate blocking your path, then you did not get the treasures in the same order as I described in the Ultima section. You will need to return to the Taurus Gate Stone, activate it, and run over here. If there is no gate, then proceed and take the only available exit. You will find Way Stone XXI. Take it to Way Stone XXII, which will have only one exit leading to Way Stone XXIII. This will transport you to Way Stone XXIV, and when you step in the next zone you will be able to see Omega Mark XII.

> ##### Boss: Omega Mark XII
>
>
> | **Level** | 99 | **LP** | 255 | **CP** | 65000 |
> | **HP** | 1037069 | **Steal** | Knot of Rust (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Varies |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | Varies |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> Omega Mark XII uses an attack that can easily wipe out a character in one or two hits, and does so rapidly. I tried several super-complicated strategies that seemed like cool solutions to how dangerous and fast Omega Mark XII is. None of them worked. So I finally settled on the tried-and-true Reverse + Bubble Belt + Wither strategy and the battle was a breeze. I came here directly after Facing Yiazmat. I use my Knight/Bushi, Foebreaker/Shikari, and White Mage/Machinist as two damage dealers and one support character.
>
> To prepare for battle, have all three characters wearing Bubble Belts, and have your support character castReverse on the other two. Protectga is probably worthwhile as well. For your two attackers, they only need the gambits 1) Ally: any, Phoenix Down and 2) Foe: any, Attack. One of your attackers should also have the gambit Foe: any, Wither set up below the Phoenix Down gambit. Your support character needs to have an Arise gambit at the top, followed by Reverse gambits for each of the attackers. Below that, a Hi-Ether gambit (MP < 20% should be fine), followed by Protectga and Bravery for each of the attackers. Do not have an attack gambit for your support character.
>
> Start the battle by having your Wither-user as party leader and running in while under the effects of Reverse. As soon as that character is engaged, switch party leaders to the support characters and try to stay just barely in range to keep Reverse up. Until Omega reaches HP critical, it typically won't target anyone not nearby. Just in case, be prepared to cast Cura **ga** on yourself. If your support character keeps getting targeted, add a Decoy gambit for one of your attackers.
>
> Expect your attackers to die fairly regularly at the beginning of the battle as they take damage from attacking Omega (which doesn't get reversed). Once you see that the attackers are getting healed for less than 1000 HP whenever they are getting attacked (thanks to Wither), turn of the Reverse gambits and the Wither gambit. As soon as Reverse wears off, turn on a Curaja gambit in the same place as your Reverse gambits used to be. At this point, the battle should go fairly smoothly.
>
> At HP critical, Omega will start targeting your support character, even from a distance. If your support character gets Berserk status inflicted, manually have one of your attackers use a Dispel Mote on your support character.

After the battle, be sure to pick up the loot bag as it holds the Omega Badge. This, along with the badge you receive from defeating [Yiazmat](#optional---endgame-sidequests) and catching the King of the Nebra 10 times in the [Fishing Sidequest](#fishing-sidequest) can be sold to craft the Wyrmhero Blade in the Bazaar.

### Trophy cleanup

At this point, you should have almost every trophy in the game. Throughout the walkthrough, I listed where I received each trophy. Other than the platinum, all that remained were:

If you want to complete the bestiary the way the you had to in the original game, you may be lacking some rare game or normal monsters that only appear in certain weather condition. Check out my [bestiary list](#lists-for-trophies) to compare yours to and get some tips on monsters that are harder to find.

If you are struggling with activating every concurrence, it helps to control how many Quickening licenses you've activate. There are several strategies that people suggest. I have my own that I cover in my [Concurrence list](#lists-for-trophies).

After earning both of those, you should have all 30 items unlocked in the Sky Pirate's Den and can now receive the reward from Montblanc for reaching Order of Ambrosia rank in [Clan Centurio](#clan-centurio---the-complete-list): _Centurio Hero's Badge_ and _3 Teleport Stones_. At this rank, the Clan Provisioner will now sell Dark Matter as well as most Motes. Dark Matter is a potentially incredible item if you like to use Knots of Rust. The more Knots of Rust you've used, the more damage Dark Matter will do - up to 60,000 damage. Unfortunately, every time you use a Dark Matter, the counter starts over. The damage is one-third the total amount of damage dealt by Knots of Rust since the last time you used a Dark Matter. Shemhazai's skill Devour Soul counts toward your Knot of Rust damage, and the skill Soul Purge counts as using a Dark Matter. If you plan on doing Trial Mode, having a Dark Matter ready to deal 60,000 damage in stock for Trial 100 is definitely helpful.

The [Trial Mode](#trial-mode) trophies should be fairly straightforward to obtain, especially if you are a high level. Hah...just kidding. Trials 1-86 are a breeze. Trial 87, 99, and 100 are a nightmares. I give some preparation recommendations and some tips, but if you really want these trophies some video walkthroughs may serve you best - just being honest.

Wings of My Own will be covered in the final portion of the story walkthrough and should be the last trophy you need to get Platinum! If you are missing any of the other trophies, check out the section of [Lists for Trophies](#lists-for-trophies) to see what you might be missing.

## Walkthrough Part 6 - Finale

#### Sky Fortress Bahamut

| **Treasures** | 1 ( **Antechamber - East**), 1 ( **Antechamber - North**), 1 ( **Antechamber - West**), 1 ( **Antechamber - South**) |
| **Notable Loot** | 1) Dark Matter, X-Potion, or Scathe Mote; 2) Elixir, X-Potion, or Scathe Mote; 3) Hastega Mote, X-Potion, or Scathe Mote; 4) Bubble Mote, X-Potion, or Scathe Mote |
| **Enemies** | Imperial Swordsman, Imperial Gunner, Mastiff, Judge, Imperial Pilot, Imperial Beastmaster, Spinner-Rook, Sphere-Rook, Helm Rook, Judge Gabranth (boss), Vayne (boss), Vayne Novus (boss), Sephira, The Undying (boss) |

Board the Strahl from an aerodrome or one of the world map locations where it anchors. Select _Bahamut_, which is adjacent to Rabanastre. When you disembark, you will have no map to guide you and it just says you are in **Antechamber**. For orientation, let's call this **Antechamber - East**. In front of you is a treasure. All of the treasure will hold an X-Potion or a Scathe Mote if you have a Diamond Armlet equipped. Without it, this one holds a Dark Matter. If you turn right, you'll face north. If you turn left, you'll face south. There are eight zones around the edge: 4 Antechambers (East, North, West, and South) and four Peripheries at the corners. Each Antechamber holds a one-time treasure. In the north, there is an Elixir, to the west is a Hastega Mote, and to the south is a Bubble Mote. You can run around and collect them (or the X-Potion/Scathe Mote with the Diamond Armlet), or head directly to the **Catwalk** by taking the central path from any **Periphery**. You can reach the **Central Lift** from the center of the **Catwalk**.

On the **Central Lift**, find the Lift Controls to begin your ascent.

> ##### Boss: Gabranth
>
>
> | **Level** | 49 | **LP** | 25 | **CP** | 4140 |
> | **HP** | 70719 | **Steal** | Hi-Potion (55/80%), X-Potion (10/30%), Elixir (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Silence, Slow |
>
> As with most bosses, kick things off with Dispel. Keep Protect up and have an Arise gambit (or Raise or Phoenix Down, if you don't have Arise). If you have Basch in the party, you won't have to cast Decoy because Gabranth will stay focused there. This allows you buff up Basch's defense, give him Bravery with a good combo weapon like the Kumbha, and have Basch and a strong magick caster beat up on Gabranth.
>
> Gabranth will gain all his HP back about halfway through the battle by using Renew and start doing more damage with the skill Innocence. Mainly, it's still the same as the first part of the battle. If Gabranth is doing too much damage, have someone come in and use Wither a few times. Alternatively, you could cast Blind. Whatever you need to stay healthy.

After the battle, use the lift and be prepared for a series of three boss battles.

> ##### Boss: Vayne
>
>
> | **Level** | 50 | **LP** | 0 | **CP** | 0 |
> | **HP** | 76755 | **Steal** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Silence, Sap |
>
> Vayne is a physical fighter. Until his HP drops below 50%, shields are effective. Given that he can combo up to 11 times, the shields can make a difference. Bubble Belts are helpful since you don't have to worry about status effects. Obviously, keep Arise, Curaja, and Protectga at the top of your healer's gambits. Bravery on any melee and Faith on any caster will help speed things up. Just like with Gabranth, use Wither and take advantage of Vayne's susceptibility to Blind if you are struggling. Try to finish the battle in good health as you still have more battles to go.

> ##### Boss: Vayne Novus
>
>
> | **Level** | 53 | **LP** | 0 | **CP** | 0 |
> | **HP** | 104210 | **Steal** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Dark |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Oil, Slow |
>
> This battle is much different than the previous. Vayne Novus has the use of the five elemental -ga spells, making Shellga as important as Protectga. At 65% HP, he will use Inviolable Will and will then become immune to magick for a time. He also has the ability to restore HP using Vortices. If you want to take out the five accompanying Sephira, use an AoE spell like Scourge or Scathe. They only have around 12,000 HP and should go down quickly with a couple of casts. You may want to change any melee characters attack gambit to target Foe: Highest max HP so they don't waste time chasing the Sephira. Let your casters handle them. Again, if the damage from Vayne Novus is too high, use Wither and Addle to knock him down a bit.

> ##### Boss: The Undying
>
>
> | **Level** | 55 | **LP** | 0 | **CP** | 0 |
> | **HP** | 228299 | **Steal** | Dark Matter (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> The Undying will erect barriers that make it immune to physical or magickal damage, so be attentive and swap gambits accordingly. Your best bet is a dedicated healer / buff caster, Scathe for when magickal damage gets through, and a high combo sword like the Kumbha for when physical damage gets through. Try to keep Bubble up in preparation for the Teraflare. It can do 6000 damage to a high-level party. If you notice you are getting hit with status effects, equip Ribbons or any accessories that handle your least favorite. Have a Renew gambit ready for recovery after the big attacks and The Undying will eventually let its guard down and you'll finish it off.

## Trial Mode

Trial Mode can be entered from the games Main Menu at any time using an existing party by loading its save file after selecting Trial Mode. While in Trial Mode, you will have all of the levels, gear, items, gambits, etc. that your party had in the save file you used. While in Trial Mode, you will create an autosave after every level you complete, and you have the option to create a permanent save every ten levels. If you load a Trial Mode save or autosave into the main game, you will resume the game right where you were with the file used for Trial Mode. Any items used or gained in Trial Mode, however, will carry over. This is how you can get filthy rich and overpowered early in the game.

If you want to complete trial mode, you have to do 100 trials from a single spot. If you load your Trial Mode save into your game, you can always go back and do Trial Mode from that point (assuming you don't overwrite the file), but you cannot progress further in the game or go farm items you need or anything like that without completely restarting Trial Mode. So if you reach Trial 100 and only have 2 Elixirs and no Dark Energy...you probably aren't going to make it. If you don't like how you performed since the last hard save, though, you can always back up and use a previous Trial Mode save file that you you haven't overwritten to try again.

All that being said, there are some items that you want to have extremely well stocked if you plan on making it through all 100 trials.

- 99 Prince's Kiss
- 99 X-Potions
- 99 Phoenix Downs
- 99 Remedies
- 99 Bacchus's Wine
- 25 Elixirs or more, or 10 Dark Energy
- 10-15 Dispel Motes
- 1 or 2 Nihopalaoa
- 3 Ribbons
- 3 Bubble Belts
- Zodiark licensed to your Black Mage

There are a few places to grab Elixirs as you go through Trial Mode, but Dark Energy is extremely hard to come by. If you don't mind the RNG manipulation, that is the easiest way to get them from **Cerobi Steppe**. This guide, however, was written for anybody. I only had 1 Dark Energy that I probably got from the Bazaar - but honestly didn't even know I had it until failing Trial 100 several times and getting desperate to find anything in my items that would help. Boy did it (50,000 damage to all enemies in range!). I reached Trial 100 with about 15 Elixirs and had 2 left after winning.

If you are attempting to do the whole thing, I would wait until you are pretty much through with the entire game, including all sidequests. The experience (literal experience, not the experience used for leveling up) you get from fighting all of the marks and optional bosses is going to be needed to get through the last several Trials. You will be facing Hell Wyrm, Yiazmat, and Omega Mark XII again and they aren't really any easier.

If you are just going for the trophies once you've completed the game, expect the first 50 Trials to go by quick. Have a tank / physical attacker, a black magick caster with gambits for the different elemental weaknesses and Scourge for anyone else, and a healer with Arise and Curaja or Renew. Equip your Ribbons and don't blink or you'll miss it. Even after Trial 50, there isn't much challenge until you get to the Entites on Trials 73-80. Trial 86, however, is where you will have to start really playing around with your strategies. From then on, if you don't know what's coming expect to party-wipe pretty quickly. Even then, in many cases you have to set up for the next Trial before you finish the one you're in or you could be in trouble. That is what made Trial 100 so difficult for me...

If you are playing Trial Mode for goodies, I'll try to make a list of what interesting things you can get from the different levels. Keep in mind that you need a Diamond Armlet equipped for all the good stuff. A few are actually really worthwhile. I'll be honest, though, you won't find anything better than the Trial Mode walkthrough on Jegged: https://jegged.com/Games/Final-Fantasy-XII/Trial-Mode/. I will say that the strategy for Trial 100 on Jegged never came even close to working for me. The only strategy that seemed reasonable required the use of the RNG manipulation to farm Dark Energy, so I worked out a variant that didn't require Dark Energy but consumed a bunch of Elixirs. If you are playing for goodies, however, Trial 100 doesn't matter. You can't keep anything you get from Trial 100 as there is no Autosave File created when it is done, nor is there a chance to do a permanent Save File. You do get the opportunity to create a New Game-, however. In New Game-, your characters never level up, adding a _slight_ challenge to the game.

Now that we've discussed Trial Mode a bit, let's get started!

### Trials 1-10

I will only list the noteworthy loot. Most of it is more easily obtained by playing through the game. The Diamond Armlet in Trial 1 is the only treasure loot listed that doesn't require a Diamond Armlet equipped.

| Trial | Enemies | Loot in treasures | Loot from foes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 6 Dire Rats | Diamond Armlet |  |
| 2 | Thextera, Alpha Wolf, Hyena |  |  |
| 3 | Flowering Cactoid, Cactoids, Cactites<br>(Ripe Rampager can be spawned by going to the lower level) |  | Karkata (rare steal from Flowering Cactoid) |
| 4 | Razorfin, 4 Ichthons |  |  |
| 5 | Nekhbet, Werewolf, 4 Cockatrices |  |  |
| 6 | Cluckatrice, 4 Chickatrices |  |  |

### Trials 61-70

If you haven't already, be sure to have Ribbons equipped from here on out. It is best to keep Bubble cast, as well. This can help fend off the Disease status and help deal with overwhelming damage output that can come from the Baknamys in Trial 61 and the Pugilists in Trial 66. Don't forget to cast Berserk on the Vorpal Bunny in Trial 62 if it starts to get away from you and you've already gotten the Zodiac Spear. Be prepared for not being able to use items during Trial 68.

### Trials 71-80

Trials 71 and 72 have a lot of good gear available. Trials 73 through 80 have great items to steal from the Entites. It is critical to have good elemental gambits set up on your Black Mage along with Ribbons on all your characters. If you pay attention, you can also swap out gear to make sure that your Entite and Elemental foes will almost always be healing you.
