### Trials 81-85

The fights should start becoming challenging now.

In Trial 81, you cannot use magick, so swap gambits to being item and technick heavy. Equip Holy weapons to speed things along.

In Trial 84, you cannot use the attack command. Swap gambits to use magick and technicks. The Chaosjets seemed to keep respawning, as well. AoE spells meant that Chaos continually took damage anyway and still fell fairly quickly. You will have to be careful about your elemental gambits and be sure you stay focused on one element at a time until it's destroyed.

There is a lot of good armor to be had in these stages, as well as several Elixirs. If you've been using Elixirs, you definitely want to try to restock and start saving for Trial 100.

| Trial | Enemies | Loot in treasures | Loot from foes |

| 85 | Pylraster, Abelisk, Terror Tyrant |  | Renewing Morion (uncommon steal from Pylraster), Brave Suit (rare steal from Pylraster), Maximillian (stolen from Abelisk), Magepower Shishak (stolen from Terror Tyrant), Grand Helm (poached from Terror Tyrant) |

### Trial 86-90

Trial 86 was the first trial that I failed on my first attempt as I was trying all of these blindly. It took a few attempts to find a strategy that worked. Ribbons are an absolute must. Raz (who you cannoot target) will constantly try to afflict your party with status ailments. Elza will heal the others and cast Reverse. Stopping her is a priority and can be done with a Bacchus's Wine. It doesn't last forever, so be ready to hit her with another if necessary. Rikken can be dealt with by using Wither several times until it feels like he's hitting you with a feather instead of a 2 x 4. The Pirates can be dispatched fairly quickly with a Warp Mote (I needed two to get rid of them all). With the Pirates gone, Elza out of her mind, and Rikken rendered impotent, the stage isn't so bad. If Reverse gets applied, be sure to remove it with Dispel.

Trial 87 is almost identical to the fight against Hell Wyrm in **Sochen Cave Palace**. Ribbons can prevent Stop, White Masks can absorb Holy, and Wither and Addle can really help lower the Hell Wyrm's danger level. HP critical is still difficult to deal with. Like before, make sure that you have at least one character that is always dealing damage and have your healer set up casting Renew.

Trial 88 has you against the most formidable of all the rare game: Disma. Be sure to dispel the Reflect status and then go to town with Excalibur, Holy, Scourge, etc. You only have to defeat Disma to proceed. I set all gambits to target foe with highest max HP. It felt like Disma stayed at 1% HP for a very long time, but he eventually fell. It might be wiser to take out the lesser foes, but if you are quick it might not matter.

Trial 89 has another foe that can inflict lots of bad status effects. Be sure those Ribbons stay equipped. Wither and Addle are essential for making this fight more manageable. Expose and Shear can also be helpful once you have the damage output under control. Scourge is the best magick for the job.

Trial 90 felt like a repeat of Trial 89 with the exception of palings preventing physical and magickal damage, alternating every few minutes. The strategy is about the same as Trial 89.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| 87 | Hell Wyrm |  | Excalibur (rare steal) |

| 89 | Fafnir |  | Grand Helm (stolen), Grand Armor (poached) |

**Reward for completing stage 90:** 9000 gil, 2 Megalixirs, 15 Remedies.

### Trials 91-95

Trial 91 was the second trial I failed. I recognized the Magick Pot as needing an Elixir, but my Black Mage was healing it constantly by targeting the Hecteyes elemental weakness to Lightning. The Magick Pot absorbs ALL elements and eventually began attacking me. Once I switched off all element-based gambits, had my Black Mage casting Scourge, and had all of my characters target the Magick Pot, the fight went quickly.

In Trial 92, you get to fight Shadowseer with all of his pets at once. If you have any Hi-Ether gambits set up, turn them off. Rely on items for healing for the first part of the battle as Shadowseer will frequently eliminate all your MP. I preferred taking out Shadowseer quickly so I could get back to my normal fighting mode. Once Shadowseer was gone, the battle wasn't tough.

The Chocobos in Trial 93 can be dangerous if they start using Choco Comet. This can be prevented by putting them in Berserk status. If you want some time to steal from them, make sure to use Wither a few times so they don't sting as bad when they scratch you.

Gilgamesh will want to inflict statuses again, so Ribbons are almost necessary unless you just happen to have the perfect level that no statuses will hit. Bubble and Protect are also almost essential. In fact, if you just can't seem to get through this battle, it may be worth equipping Bubble Belts instead of Ribbons and be prepared to cast Esuna or use Remedies. Get a couple of Withers on Enkidu, then switch to Expose and try to take the dog out quickly. Once Enkidu is gone, Wither Gilgamesh several times, then Expose, and he'll fall in turn.

Ultima came very close to being the third level I failed. There is a cycling Magick Barrier that causes different difficulties in the battle. Be sure to turn of all gambits for restoring MP while MP drain is up. Be sure to switch away from the attack command while it is sealed. Similarly, switch from magicks with the barrier shifts to no magicks. Once the barrier prevents technicks, try your best to finish the battle quickly as it is the best breather you get. Toss some Hi-Ethers if necessary and then do your worst. Be sure that you remove the Reverse status from any characters by using Dispel or a Dispel Mote - but don't use too many of your Dispel Motes. You will need them in Trial 99.

| 92 | Shadowseer, Fenrir, Slyt, Pandaemonium, Phoenix |  | Elixir (stolen from Shadowseer) |
| 93 | 3 Lv.99 Red Chocobos |  | Elixir (common steal), Megalixir (uncommon steal), Ribbon (rare steal) |

| 95 | Ultima | Ether | White Mask (common steal), White Robes (uncommon steal), Lordly Robes (rare steal) |

### Trial 96

Trial 96 has you face a seemingly endless supply of Abysteel. When I faced them, it completely cycled through the alphabet twice and was early in the third time through when they quit spawning. I faced at least 74, maybe more. The trick to this battle is to have one person with a Sleepga gambit targeting any foe, while everyone else has their gambits off. This allows you to control the flow of the battle and take breathers if necessary. You should kill the bats by manually casting Curaja or having someone with Excalibur take them out one at a time. When one is killed, another will spawn in its place.

The reason for the random number of Abysteel is that you really need to be ready for the next battle - and you can't do that if you don't know when this stage is going to end. At some point, you want to get your two strongest melee fighters holding non-elemental high-combo weapons fully buffed (Shell, Bubble, Protect, Haste, and Bravery) and berserked and put them in your reserve party. You will also want you healer in your reserve party too, although that can be tricky to pull off. When you think you are down to the last Abysteel, make sure it is asleep, cast Disable on it, and then finish it off with only one active party member that you don't mind dying in the next battle. The next Trial will open up with magick being cast that will likely KO anyone in the active party.

### Trial 97

Zodiark opens the battle with the Darkja attack that deals Dark damage and has a high probability of KOing anyone it hits. Hopefully you followed the advice from the last Trial and only one character gets KOed. Bring in your healer with a good Renew gambit and your two buffed, berserked fighters. Keep Bravery gambits up to keep them wailing away. Be sure to revive your fallen comrade early in the battle while you are still healthy. Zodiark fell very quickly for me.

If you feel like risking it, you can steal a Black Mask (common), Black Robes (uncommon), and Lordly Robes (rare) from Zodiark. If you don't kill it quickly enough once it reaches 20% HP, however, it essentially stays immune to damage.

### Trial 98

Yiazmat fortunately does not have over 50 million HP in stage 98. The fight still follows almost the exact same pattern. Yiazmat likes to use Cyclone and its physical attacks all have a chance to KO. Ribbons can help prevent Stop, and Shell can help diminish the chances of KO. Start the battle by using Wither and Addle until they connect at least 5 times and you only see misses. After that, Shear and Expose. Keep your healer away from Yiazmat's physical range and keep up Bubble, Protect, Shell, and Bravery (or Faith for a caster). Yiazmat seemed to be much more successful at connecting with Death Strike and this also came very close to being the third failed Trial. Given the number of times characters will get KOed, it may be preferable to have Bubble Belts equipped and cast Esuna or use Remedies as the need arises instead of equipping Ribbons.

You can steal a Yagyu Darkblade (uncommon) or Mesa (rare) from Yiazmat.

To prepare for the next phase, have at least one character that can use Wither in your active party and be sure that no one is in Berserk status. You will want a White Mage and possibly another support character. All three should equip Bubble Belts and have an Arise / Phoenix Down gambit at the top of the list. Your attacker should have a Wither gambit set pretty high and ready to be turned on, and your White Mage should have a Renew gambit for any ally with less than 70% HP ready to be turned on. You will also need gambits set just below the Arise / Phoenix Down gambit that casts Dispel on Berserked allies or uses a Dispel Mote on the healer should that character be Berserked. All should have as high of a defense as you can get, and try to get them all with Protect status on before finishing off Yiazmat.

### Trial 99

As soon as the battle starts, turn on all those gambits mentioned previously. Start taking down Omega Mark XII's attack damage using Wither and keep casting Renew after every hit. Eventually, the damage will become very manageable (only about 500 per hit). At that point, you may be able to bring in a second attacker and focus more on offense rather than defense. Don't drop the Dispel gambits for Berserked allies. You can use Expose to help speed the battle along. If you need MP, most of the traps around the room are Manafont traps and will restore your MP. Since some can do some serious damage, don't push your luck until Omega is fully debuffed. Some of the treasures contain Elixirs when you don't have a Diamond Armlet equipped, as well. If you do have the Diamond Armlet equipped, each treasure has a 5% chance of containing a Megalixir.

The Ribbon is an uncommon steal from Omega Mark XII.

Once the battle get close to being over, you will want to slow down or stop attacking so you can prepare for the last stage (keep on healing, however). You will want to go into the next stage with a White Mage, a Black Mage with Zodiark licensed, and a high defense character. Your Mages need to have Ribbons equipped, while your defense person needs to equip a Nihopalaoa. Be sure that nobody is berserked and that all three have Protect and Shell on. Also have Faith on your mages. If you can get Haste on them all, go for it. When ready, finish off Zodiark.

FYI, I believe I went into my menu right as I dealt the final blow to Omega Mark XII. As a consequence, the game crashed when I tried to access the gambit menu and I got to replay Trial 99. The battle went very smoothly the second time around as my Wither connected much faster - but I was paranoid about another crash and completely failed to get buffs set up for the next battle. I didn't know what to expect, either, and had no idea about the strategy I'm about to present to you. That should speak to how effective the strategy is. I entered Trial 100 without any of the proper gambits in place, the characters I ended up using had NO buffs (not even Bubble), and one of my characters used Libra right at the beginning and I had to wait for that animation to finish before I could even swap that character for one that I needed. The autosave feature really did me in. After several different failed strategies, I finally worked out a simple strategy that worked great. I have since learned of some others that may be even better - if you are almost level 99. I was in the high 70s, and this strategy should be sufficient for anyone level 70 or above - even if you fail to prepare well in Trial 99.

### Trial 100

Trial 100 pits you against the five judges: Ghis, Gabranth, Zagrabaath, Bergan, and Drace. Zargabaath uses Elixirs, fully restoring the HP of damaged allies, making this fight seemingly impossible given that Ghis has the fewest HP at 123,244. Given how hard Bergan and Gabranth hit, if you can't take them all down quickly or at least incapacitate some, they will make quick work of any setup you choose.

The goal in this Trial is to have your Black Mage repeatedly summon Zodiark and immediately use its technick Final Eclipse. Someone with high defense equipped with a Nihopalaoa keeps Zagrabaath asleep by using Prince's Kiss on him. Your last character will be casting Renew repeatedly to keep the other two alive. The gambit setup needs to be extremely simple so you don't get distracted from your main tasks, because if you get sidetracked you will fail.

If at all possible, come into this battle buffed: Protect, Shell, Bubble, Haste, and maybe even Faith on your Black Mage. Your Black Mage and White Mage need to be wearing Ribbons and have as much defense as possible. Your tank should be wearing a Nihopalaoa and have as much defense as possible.

Your Black Mage needs two gambits:

- Ally: any » Phoenix Down
- Foe: lowest HP » Scathe

You will want your Black Mage to be party leader and you will need to be constantly running from any foes targeting you, but staying within range of your healer. Only summon Zodiark if you are not in imminent danger of being attacked. If your Black Mage is killed while Zodiark is present, Zodiark just gets dismissed. As long as you are not about to be hit and have max HP, summon Zodiark and IMMEDIATELY tell it to use Final Eclipse. Target Ghis first until he is knocked out, then Drace, then Bergan, then Gabranth. If done correctly, Zagrabaath will NEVER wake up and you can finish Zagrabaath with Scathe once the other four are gone. As soon as Final Eclipse finishes or your Scathe animation finishes, manually use an Elixir and repeat. Never stop moving away from your enemies. Make them waste turns chasing you and hope they don't get a major combo in.

Until Ghis and Drace are knocked out, you will have to also manually control your Nihopalaoa wearer. This person should have a good shield and strong armor. The first action should be to use a Prince's Kiss on Zagrabaath. If you have any Dark Energy, use one on Ghis. Then alternate using Prince's Kiss on Zagrabaath and Ghis. Once Ghis and Drace are gone, you can set up the gambit:

- Foe: lowest HP » Prince's Kiss

From then on, that is all this character is doing. It doesn't hurt to manually try a Prince's Kiss on Bergan after one or two on Zagrabaath. Whatever you do don't forget that this character is wearing a Nihopalaoa. They MUST NOT use restorative items on your party - including Phoenix Downs.

Your healer needs the following gambits:

- Ally: any » Arise
- Ally: HP < 70% » Renew
- Foe: any » Dispelga

Hopefully you will get a Dispelga off quickly so that Drace will be busy casting status boosters to ever cast Shock on your party. Once Drace goes down, you can switch the last gambit to Ally: any » Protectga.

Do not let your guard down once Ghis and Drace go down. Bergan and Gabranth have more than enough attack power and combo frequency to take out your Black Mage if they both manage to hit simultaneously and you are too far from your healer. Some strategies recommend using Wither a few times. My experience was that was just enough of a distraction from my main strategy to cause me to fail.

If you are interested in the loot from the level that you can't carry with you:

| Loot in treasures | Loot to steal |
| --- | --- |
| Cameo Belt<br>Jade Collar<br>Ribbon | Circlet (from Ghis)<br>Genji Gloves (from Bergan)<br>Tournesol (from Gabranth) |

Order of Ambrosia also requires that you have unlocked all 30 items in the Sky Pirate's Den. Each of the items in the Sky Pirate's Den is associated with one of the [trophies](#trophies). Specifically, you need to have earned all trophies other than story line, Trial Mode, and Platinum.

#### The Rewards

In addition to rewards for defeating assigned marks, Montblanc will also reward you after defeating key enemies in the game. These are given in the order they are accomplished, regardless of when you turn them in - so the order you receive them may be different than the order I received them.

| --- | --- |

| Defeat Firemane | 200 gil |
| Defeat Mimic Queen | 300 gil |
| Defeat optional Demon Wall | 1200 gil<br>Electrum (x1) |
| Defeat mandatory Demon Wall | 600 gil<br>Warp Mote (x1) |
| Control one Esper | Arcana (x2)<br>Teleport Stone (x2) |
| Defeat Earth Tyrant | 1200 gil |
| Defeat Ahriman | 1600 gil |
| Defeat Mandragoras | 1600 gil |

| Defeat Daedalus | 1900 gil |
| Defeat Tyrant | 1900 gil |

| Defeat King Bomb | 1300 gil<br>Mallet (x1) |
| Control four Espers | High Arcana (x1)<br>Teleport Stone (x2) |
| Defeat Fury | 2100 gil<br>Bacchus's Wine |
| Defeat Humbaba Mistant | 2100 gil |
| Control eight Espers | Gemsteel (x1)<br>Teleport Stone (x2) |

## Lists for Trophies

### Scrivener - Completed the Bestiary

While the trophy description seems to imply that you have to open up every page of lore on every creature, that is fortunately not true. All you need to do is to defeat every enemy in the game at least once. Okay, so "all you need to do" isn't that trivial. Still, it's better than defeating every enemy enough times to unlock all the lore.

Some things to keep in mind - not all "common" enemies are common. Many enemies require certain conditions (such as specific weather) to appear. If there are any special spawn conditions, they will be noted in the "Notes" column on the right. Furthermore, while I'll be listing the bestiary as it appears in the game, sometimes you will encounter a creature first or more easily in a different region than the game categorizes them. A simple example is the Urstrix, which can be found in Dalmasca Westersand commonly, but is listed as Giza Plains where it also appears.

If you plan on getting Platinum Trophy, it may be beneficial to play through Trial Mode before tracking down any missing entries as a fair portion can be found there. Some require certain conditions to be met in Trial Mode for them to appear, however, so I wouldn't go too far out of my way to get every beast in trial mode first.

The table below will have the creatures listed in two columns to match what you would see in game. Details for Espers, bosses, Rare Game, and Marks will be linked to where they are covered in the walkthrough. If not covered elsewhere, details will be put in the Notes column.

### Runeweaver - Learned every Magick

There are 81 magicks in the game spread across 5 disciplines: [White](#lists-for-trophies)(25), [Black](#lists-for-trophies)(25), [Time](#lists-for-trophies)(20), [Green](#lists-for-trophies)(6), and [Arcane](#lists-for-trophies)(5).

### Jack-of-All-Trades - Learned every Technick

Most technicks are either available for purchase or are found in one-time guaranteed chests. Only two are in low frequency chests. To check which technicks you already have, you can go to the license board and press square twice, or you can go to your gambit setup and look for any gaps in the technick page. To make checking your list easier, these are listed in the order they appear in the game list, not in the order in which they can be obtained. Jobs listed with an asterisk have the listed Technick behind an Esper or a Quickening.

| **Technick** | **Description** | **Jobs** | **Purchase Price** | **Location** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

### Cartographer - Fully Explored every Map

While the trophy description says you must fully explore every map, apparently owning a map of every area and stepping foot in every area is sufficient. To make this easier, this portion will show you how to obtain each map and give a checklist of every zone in each map that you can use to keep track. Each moogle you can buy the map from or location where you find a map will be linked to where you may encounter it in the walkthrough. Areas that you cannot return to are not required for this trophy, such as Nalbina Dungeons, Dreadnought Leviathan, and Draklor Laboratory. Zones are listed in two columns to conserve space. Commonly overlooked zones are marked with \*\*.

### Mist Walker - Performed Every Concurrence

Concurrences are final attacks that follow a series of Quickenings when certain thresholds are met. Tthe ability to reset your license board gives you much more control over what level of Quickenings each character has, and thus the likelihood of getting each Concurrence. It may still take practice and a little bit of luck for the Concurrences with the highest thresholds. In general, make sure your characters only have as many Quickenings licensed as the highest level Quickening required for the Concurrence you are going for, activate the Quickenings from top to bottom in your player list so that it is easier to spot and trigger Mist Charges, and good luck!

|  | **Charges Required** |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Concurrence** | **Level 1** | **Level 2** | **Level 3** | **Strategy**(QL = Quickening Licenses) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Inferno | 3 |  |  | 3 Chars with 1 QL |
| Catacysm | 6 |  |  | 3 Chars with 1 QL |
| Torrent | 2 | 2 |  | 2 Chars with 2 QL, 1 Char with 1 QL |
| Windburst |  | 4 |  | 3 Chars with 2 QL |
| Ark Blast | 2 | 2 | 2 | 2 Chars with 3 QL, 1 Char with 2 QL |
| Whiteout |  |  | 4 | 3 Chars with 3 QL |
| Luminescence | 3 | 3 | 3 | 3 Chars with 3 QL, start with level 1 Quickening |
| Black Hole | 4 | 4 | 4 | 3 Chars with 3 QL, start with level 1 Quickening |

For Luminescence and Black Hole, a bit of luck is needed. Starting with one of the level 1 Quickenings generally means you can pull off a couple of the low level Quickenings with your top character before needing a Mist Charge. An argument could easily be made to start with a level 2 or a level 3. It could also be argued to have one character each with a different number of licenses and try to only use the character if the option to use their highest levels shows up. Given how often you'll see level 2 and level 1 options with a character with 3 Quickenings licensed, I tend to advise against that strategy. Just find a good save crystal near a bunch of enemies or where you can zone twice quickly and keep trying until you get the luck you need.

#### Adrammelech, the Wroth

This Esper is fairly easy to obtain at level 40. To begin with, head to **Ozmone Plain / The Switchback**. Use the switchback in the center of the zone to access **Zertinan Caverns**.

| **Treasures** | 6 ( **Halls of Ardent Darkness**), 4 ( **The Undershore**) |
| **Enemies** | Mallicant, Silicon Tortoise, Shambling Corpse, Adrammelech (Esper) |

Navigating the **Halls of Ardent Darkness** and **The Undershore** is not too difficult. There are a few dead ends and loops, but the maps are small. The six treasure barrels in **Halls of Ardent Darkness** may contain a Miter, Iron Pole, Magoroku, Slasher, Main Gauche, and Flametongue. The four in **The Undershore** contain restorative items or motes. There is a fifth one-time chest with a 25% spawn rate with Thief's Cuffs, but is not in the portion you are going through. Before you proceed further, be set up for the fight ahead as it will begin automatically after you take a few steps into **Athroza Quicksands**.

> ##### Esper: Adrammelech
>
>
> | **Level** | 39 | **LP** | 42 | **CP** | 4050 |
> | **HP** | 39630 | **Steal** | Pebble (55/80%), Capricorn Gem (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Ice |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Lightning<br>\[Nullify\] Fire, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Slow |
>
> Throughout the battle, Shambling Corpse will come out of the ground. Eventually, if you take long enough, they may stop spawning. If you have a gambit set up to cast Blizzara or Blizzaga on an foe that is ice-vulenrable, you can ignore the Shambling Corpse spawns by having the rest have gambits set to attack the foe with the highest max HP. Adrammelech is flying, however, so you must use ranged fighters or spears and poles.
>
> Adrammelech casts a lot of lightning-based magick, so equipping Diamond Armlets can be a huge help for your defense. At 50% health, Adrammelech will cast Thundaja. This can deal massive damage and inflict Stop. After casting Thundaja, he will use Perfect Defense, making him immune to damage for two minutes. Use the time to heal up and clear out any Shambling Corpses. If all of your party got inflicted with stop, as soon as possible swap one out for a different character and use Chronos Tears. As his health continues to drop, he will begin spamming his lightning magicks faster. If possible, string together a long Quickening chain once he drops below 20% to finish the battle before he overcomes your healing capacity.

If you came here from the walkthrough, where did you leave off?

- [Doing sidequests after the Draklor Laboratory raid.](#optional---post-draklor-sidequests-and-hunts)
- After getting _The Strahl_

#### Zalera, the Death Seraph

If you did the Bloodwing hunt, you should have progressed most of the way to Zalera already. From Bloodwing, continue into **Terminus No. 7 Adjunct**. The preparation for this fight will be very bizarre. Turn off all gambits that cast healing or use healing items except Remedy. Use two characters that can cast Curaja or Curaga and equip both with Ruby Rings. Set up a gambit for each to cast Curaja or Curaga on the other (but don't turn it on until the fight begins). If possible, have both of them set up to use Charge if their MP drops below 10%. Also have each character with a gambit to use Phoenix Down or cast Raise at the top of the list. The strategy here is to reflect healing onto the undead Esper and spawns as much as possible on Zalera and the Dead Bones that pop up, but not bothering with any healing of yourself. When ready, head into **Terminus No. 7** and proceed down the hall to start the battle.

> ##### Esper: Zalera
>
>
> | **Level** | 40 | **LP** | 40 | **CP** | 3600 |
> | **HP** | 72248 | **Steal** | Pebble (55/80%), Gemini Gem (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Nullify\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind, Slow |
>
> You only have five minutes to finish this battle, which is why you can't waste precious time trying to heal after every resurrection. Zalera likes to use the skill Kill which is an instant KO, so you will often be short HP. While Dead Bones are around, Zalera is virtually impervious to damage - except reflected cure magicks. By having Curaga and Curaja reflected, you can damage through the barrier AND wipe out the Dead Bones very quickly.
>
> If you wish, Dispel Zalera's starting statuses. Turn on your gambits to start casting Curaja and Curaga. If you wish to steal, you may want to do it manually so that you don't waste a bunch of time trying to steal from Dead Bones. Eventually, the Dead Bones will stop spawning and your third character will begin doing damage. If you feel the need or your time is running out, chain together some Quickenings to help finish the battle in time.

If you came here from the walkthrough, where did you leave off?

- [Directly after killing Bloodwing](#optional---post-draklor-sidequests-and-hunts)
- After getting _The Strahl_

#### Cúchulainn, the Impure

| **Notable Loot** | Hornito, Jade Collar, Gilt Measure, Achilles (technick) |
| **Enemies** | Lizard, Water Elemental, Baritine Croc, Gespenst, Drowned (rare game) Cúchulainn (esper) |

In order to reach this esper, you must be able to manipulate the Garamsythe Waterway controls with the Sluice Key. This requires having completed the hunts for the Wraith and the White Mousse. With the Sluice Key in hand, head to **Garamsythe Waterway / Central Waterway Control**. Start by closing only the No. 10 and No. 3 controls on the west side. With the No. 10 closed, you can reach the lowest level in the **No. 10 Channel**, which holds a one-time guaranteed Hornito in the big open chamber on the west side. From **Central Waterway Control**, head down **No. 3 Cloaca Spur**. Use the No. 1 South Waterway Control, then look for a one-time guaranteed Jade Collar on the lowest level below the control mechanism. Head back to **Central Waterway Control** and open 10 and 3 back up, but close No. 11 and No. 4. With No. 11 closed, you can reach the lowest levels in the **No. 11 Channel**, which holds a one-time guaranteed Gilt Measure in the area mirrored where you found the Hornito. Return to **Central Waterway Control** and head southeast into **No. 4 Cloaca Spur**. This time activate the No. 1 North Waterway Control and look for a one-time guaranteed treasure down below with the Achilles technick. If you hang out here for five to eight minutes, the rare game Drowned will eventually spawn on the lower level. Now head back to **Central Waterway Control**. Open No. 11, but close No. 3 again. Save your game before heading south in **No. 1 Cloaca**. The esper is on the lowest level in the south end of the zone.

> ##### Esper: Cúchulainn
>
>
> | **Level** | 45 | **LP** | 50 | **CP** | 4950 |
> | **HP** | 126165 | **Steal** | Scorpio Gem (55/80%), Elixir (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> With a party of level 53 characters, this battle was still fairly challenging. There are a few things to know going in. Disable is a real threat, so having at least two characters (or preferably all three) with Black Belts equipped is almost required. There is a constant HP depletion effect which stacks with Sap and Poison, which the esper likes to inflict. You can be dropping over a thousand HP per second. Cúchulainn likes to cast Invert, swapping HP with MP that generally kills a character given the constant HP depletion (but also regenerates MP for your healer). He also likes to cast Slowga. Finally, if those weren't enough, Foobar enemies will join the fight rapidly in the beginning and these can inflict Confuse. To counter these, you need three roles in your party: dedicated healer, dedicated Foobar remover, dedicated attacker.
>
> The dedicated healer needs to have Curaja set up as the top gambit, followed by Esuna on any ally and Charge on Self: MP <10%. The other two need to have Phoenix Downs as their top gambits, followed by X-Potion for ally: HP<20%, followed by Nu Khai Sand and Chronos Tear on any ally. If you don't have Black Belts on everyone, make sure that one character wearing one has a gambit to use a Remedy on any ally afflicted with Disable. Your dedicated Foobar remover needs to have a gambit targeting foe:undead with Firaga. This needs to come before any other attacking gambit. If you can find a way to keep a Bubble gambit in, terrific.
>
> Start the battle with Dispel to remove Haste and other beneficial effects of the esper. After that, let your gambits do their work. If you see that Slowga or Diasblega is about to be cast, running the targetted character away from the group can minimize the amount of time spent using items to restore your statuses. Once the Foobars quit spawning for good (there may be a couple of lulls), you can swap in another physical attacker as long as you have similarly good gambits set up. A Pheasant Netsuke can do wonders with those X-Potions, but makes the wearer susceptible to Disable. When the esper's HP drops below 20%, it takes much reduced damage. You may want to try to finish it off with a Quickening Chain to speed things up, becuase the longer the battle goes the more difficult staying alive becomes. Eventually the battle is won and you're treated to a creepy resignation.

#### Exodus, the Judge-Sal

| **Notable Loot** | Stamp (technick) |

This esper can only be obtained after fixing the gate in Phon Coast. After that, interacting with the various shrines causes floatweed to raise and create new paths. To get this started, speak with the Learned Man standing by the Shrine of the Northwest Wind. Then speak with the Caravaner standing by the crate south of the merchant. To start off, interact with the Shrine of the East Wind, then the Shrine of the Northeast Wind, then Shrine of the South Wind. Now take the northeast exit to **Rays of Ashen Light**. Before hopping on the chocobo, use the newly created paths to grab the Stamp technick near the first set of Worgens, and the Blood Sword just across the second floatweed path. The Stamp is one-time guaranteed, but the Blood Sword is one-time with an 80% appearance rate. Now go back and give some Gysahl Greens to the chocobo and ride it south into **Empyrean Way**. Look to your right for a new floatweed path. If the game clock is between 10 and 20 minutes, you can spawn the rare game Grey Molter, a snake-like foe, on this newly reached area. This path doesn't give access to any new treasures, but if you take it to the end there is a grass path only able to be traversed by chocobo. This takes you to **Skyreach Ridge**. Head over to the west exit and dismount. Go back into **Babbling Vale**, interact with the Shrine of the West Wind and then knock over the big boulder to create a new route into the camp. Interact with Shrine of the Northwest Wind and now the path is open to the esper. Return back to **Skyreach Bridge** via the new path and then head up the north path. The new paths allow you to cross into **Empyrean Seat**. The battle will begin as soon as you arrive.

> ##### Esper: Exodus
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 52 | **CP** | 5400 |
> | **HP** | 119060 | **Steal** | Libra Gem (55/80%), Elixir (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Sap, Slow |
>
> If your party is in the 50s level, this boss isn't too difficult. You get a couple moments notice if it begins to cast Scathe or Flare, so be sure to use that to get some separation from your party members. Scathe in particular hits everyone that the Exodus is facing and can do several thousand points of damage. As such, Bubble and Shell are very important for this battle.
>
> Once Exodus's HP drops to 20%, it becomes immune to physical damage. I even tried a Quickening Chain here and it did nothing. The quickest way to finish the boss off is to swap in a Black Mage equipped with an Opal Ring and caste Flare a few times. The Opal Ring is extremely important as Exodus will cast Reflect on itself the moment you Dispel its Reflect status. Trying to cast any magick on Exodus without having an Opal Ring equipped is suicidal. A couple of Flares, however, and Exodus will be yours.

#### Zeromus, the Condemner

To find Zeromus, you will need the Stone of the Condemner received from the Acolyte at **Mt Bur-Omisace**. Take that to **Stilshrine of Miriam / Ward of Measure** and use the Way Stone on the south end. When prompted, use the stone. This will take you to **Throne of the Vieled Gods**. Before opening the door in front of you, set up a second set of gambits that relies completely on items - no magick. It's a good idea to equip two characters with a Pheasant Netsuke and your primary melee character with a Power Armlet. Be sure to have gambits set to use Phoenix Downs and Chronos' Tears on everyone, and have your attack target the foe with the lowest HP. You may want to be level 50 or above before attempting this. When all set, cast your magick buffs like Protect and Haste, but not Bubble. Then go through the doors.

> ##### Esper: Zeromus
>
>
> | **Level** | 51 | **LP** | 55 | **CP** | 5670 |
> | **HP** | 166888 | **Steal** | Cancer Gem (55/80%), Elixir (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | N/A |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Half Damage\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> This is a battle of endurance. You cannot use magick, so you cannot Dispel Zeromus. Furthermore, Dark Lords spawn continuously throughout the battle. If you don't focus on them, they will become overwhelming. If you get four or five Dark Lords around, it might be time to use a Quickening Chain to try to thin them out a bit. Zeromus will use Stop, Slow, and gravity-based magick. The Piercing Gravija particularly can do thousands of damage, possibly wiping out one or two characters. Having Pheasant Netsuke equipped ensures that you revive your characters with a lot of HP when you use a Phoenix Down. At 50% HP, Zeromus uses Greater Barrier, extending the battle even further. Don't worry so much about attacking Zeromus, let your gambits do the work. If you are spending more time KO'ed than alive, consider putting an extra gambit on that uses an X-Potion when a character's HP is below 20%. That way fewer attacks get interrupted. Eventually, the Dark Lord spawns will become less frequent (although I never noticed them stop altogether) and you'll be able to focus more on whittling down Zeromus' HP.

#### Chaos, Walker of the Wheel

This Esper cannot be obtained until you have finished [The Medallions](#the-medallions) side quest covered so far. This quest takes you to the same locations as the Nalbina-based hunts for [Goliath](#optional---endgame-sidequests) and [Deathscythe](#optional---endgame-sidequests). The available treasures and enemy lists are covered there, if you are interested. If you did those previously, you should have all the important treasures. If you are doing these all together, the walkthrough for the hunts indicates where the important steps for obtaining this esper occur and link over here. If you are trying to obtain this esper without doing those hunts, you should at least check out where the important magick treasures are in **Nabreus Deadlands** and the **Necrohol of Nabudis**. The rest of this portion will assume that you are not going after the esper while doing those hunts.

From the Nabreus Crystal, head north into **Lifeless Strand** and work your way to the northeast corner and into **Field of the Fallen Lord**. The southeast exit leads you into **Necrohol of Nabudis** and stright onto a bunch of traps. Be sure to have Float status active or everyone wearing Steel Polyens. There is no map urn for this region, but you can obtain a map after defeating some of the bosses that have to be faced before reaching the esper. In **Hall of Slumbering Might**, the stairs to the next level are generally south east. To reach them, head south down the first hallway and take the last turn east. Follow this path east as far as you can until you have to turn south. By this point, you should see the stairwell access.

In **Hall of the Ivory Covenant**, there aren't any major branching pathways. Work your way to the center of the zone and look for the Door of Loathing in the east wall. Use your Medallion of Love to enter and face Fury

> ##### Boss: Fury
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 35 | **CP** | 2760 |
> | **HP** | 69710 | **Steal** | Blood Wool (55/80%), Moondust (10/30%), Aries Gem (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Water |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Fire |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Silence, Sap, Slow, Berserk |
>
> Like the other rabbit-type foes, Fury is fast and fury-ous. Set up a gambit to use Dispel on foe:any and this battle should go fairly easily. Fury gets dangerous at low HP, but wants to be in Berserk status where it will do thousands of damage rapidly. It will continuously waste time drinking Bacchus's Wine instead of attacking, however, if you have one person constantly dispelling the Berserk status. It's high evasion makes ranged weapons like bows and guns more effective. I don't think my axe-wielder hit more than half a dozen times through the whole battle. Fortunately, with the low HP, it doesn't take many successful hits to achieve successful hare removal. Don't miss the Golden Axe sitting on the trap in the back of the room after the battle.

After defeating Fury, head south to the find the exit to the next area, **Cloister of the Highborn**. Unless you are looking for loot, head all the way west, ignoring branches to the north and south. Once you reach the west edge, the Bravery magick is found in the room to the north and the exit to the next zone is south. in **Cloister of Distant Song**, follow the path west, turn south, and take the first path west to reach the Door of Horrors. You can use the Medallion of Bravery to open the door and face Humbaba Mistant.

> ##### Boss: Humbaba Mistant
>
>
> | **Level** | 46 | **LP** | 35 | **CP** | 6210 |
> | **HP** | 314086 | **Steal** | Beastlord Hide (55/80%), Beastlord Horn (10/30%), Behemoth Steak (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | N/A |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Silence, Sap, Slow |
>
> Humbaba Mistant is a pretty straightforward physical attacker. Use Expose a bunch of times to keep his defense low, keep up Protect, Haste, and Bubble. Consider casting Bravery on your primary attacker, and consider equipping the Holy Lance. Black Belts are useful as his attacks may inflict Disable. It has a lot of HP, but doesn't have much else going for it. There is a Scathe Mote sitting on a trap in the back of the room waiting for you after the battle.

You will receive the map of the Necrohol of Nabudis for defeating Humbaba Mistant. Return to **Cloister of the Highborn**. Work your way to the center region where you can turn south. At the south end, you will find the Door of Despair, opened with the Medallion of Might. Chaos waits behind the door.

> ##### Esper: Chaos
>
>
> | **Level** | 57 | **LP** | 60 | **CP** | 6260 |
> | **HP** | 208966 | **Steal** | Scorpio Gem (55/80%), Elixir (10/30%), High Arcana (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Earth |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Wind<br>\[Nullify\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Dark, Holy |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Sap, Slow |
>
> In this battle, your attack command is sealed. Change all of your gambits to use technicks. Telekinesis and Soul Eater works well. Chaos uses a lot of wind-based attacks, so you may consider equipping gear to protect against it. Nu Khai Sand gambits are essential as some of the wind magick causes Confuse. If possible, bring someone with Curaja, have Arise, and X-Potions set for gambits. Be ready to manually throw in a Hi-Ether, as well, since Chaos likes to use Fearga to deplete MP. Last but not least, Black Belts on everyone to prevent Disable. Other than that, it's another battle of endurance - but possibly your toughest yet.
>
> The battle starts with four Chaosjets around Chaos. You can steal Feystones from them, but it's best to wipe them out fast. I launched a Quickening attack right off and killed all but one that missed getting hit by Torrent. After that, spread your party out to minimize the impact of area spells. Since you are using technicks, nobody should need to run up or bunch close together. When Chaos's HP drops below 50%, shields stop being effective. When it drops below 20%, the attacks start coming extremely fast. Try to out-heal the damage and get attacks in when you can. After the battle, don't miss the Megalixir on the trap in the back of the room.

#### Ultima, the High Seraph

To find Ultima, you will need to navigate **The Great Crystal**. Teleport to **Giruvegan** and return to **The Great Crystal**.

| **Notable Loot** | Caldera, Caliper, Staff of the Magi, Shellga (magick), Crown of Laurels, Yagyu Darkblade, Ring of Renewal, Hastega, Artemis Bow |
| **Enemies** | Mom Bomb, Forbidden, Reaper, Necrophobe, Shadonir, Ash Wyrm, Skulwyrm, Giruveganus, Larva Eater (rare game), Evil Spirit (rare game), Ultima (esper) |

You should arrive at Way Stone VIII. When you arrive, look for the path blocked by the Scorpio Gate, and take the path just to the right of that. It will lead up to a Scorpio Gate Stone. Activate it and return back and take the now unblocked path up to Way Stone IX. Use it to teleport to Way Stone X. Take the only route out and you will reach Way Stone XX. Take the path just to the left of where you came from to reach a platform with a one-time guaranteed Caldera. Return to the platform with Way Stone XX. If you plan on facing Crystal Knight for the Hunt Club, take the route two to the left of where you are coming from. This will begin a circular loop and you will eventually end back at Way Stone XX and Crystal Knight will be present. Don't bother interacting with the Sagittarius Gate Stone yet as it will start a timer. You will need to have a clear route to get back quickly.

After defeating Crystal Knight, identify Gate Sagittarius I, then take the path just to the left back to the Sagittarius Gate Stone. Flip it and backtrack and touch Gate Sagittarius I. The new path will take you to Way Stone XI. Use it to reach Way Stone XIII.

There are two paths leading away. Take the one that descends. There will be a one-time guaranteed chest with a Caliper present and another path continuing down. This arrives at a dead-end platform that has a 25% chance of holding a one-time Staff of the Magi and a much smaller chance for a repeatable Meteorite. Keep rezoning until you get the Staff of the Magi as it is the best staff for a Black Mage in the game. Once you have it, return to Way Stone XIII. If you want a free Rubber Suit, head up the path that ascends, touch the Gemini Gate Stone, and continue along the path until you can open Gate Gemini I. There will be a platform with a guaranteed one-time Rubber Suit. If you don't care about that, take Way Stone XIII back to Way Stone XI. This time you will use the Sagittarius Gate Stone to open Gate Sagittarius II. You'll arrive at Way Stone XII, which will take you to Way Stone XIV.

Take the path that ascends up to the Leo Gate Stone. Use it and use the other exit to find and interact with Gate Leo I. Behind the gate is a platform with a one-time guaranteed Shellga magick. Return back to Way Stone XIV and continue past it. The next zone will have a guaranteed one-time Crown of Laurels. Continue on two more platforms. At this point, take the right-most of the two options. It should descend to a dead-end platform that has a 25% chance of holding a one-time Yagyu Darkblade. Be sure to spawn it and grab it. Return all the way to the Leo Gate Stone. When you activate the Leo Gate Stone this time, you will need to run all the way back to the fork, but instead of taking the right path to where the Yagyu Darkblade you need to take the center path and continue following it until you reach Gate Leo II. In order to get there in time, you may need to hold down R2 to flee past any foes that spawn. Once you've opened the gate and defeated the Ash Wyrm behind it, you have two routes available. Both lead to gate XV, two platforms away. This will take you to Way Stone XVI.

This area is a maze of Gates and Gate Stones. In summary, Libra gives you access to Capricorn, which gives you access to Virgo, which gives you access to Way Stone XVII and Aquarius, which gives you access to Taurus, which gives you access to the goodies (and the superboss Omega Mark XII). Let's start by accessing Way Stone XVII, since that is where the Save Crystal and Ultima can be found.

While at Way Stone XVI, there are three exits. Ascend the middle exit to find the Libra Gate Stone. When you get to the gate stone, orient the camera so that it points at the route you came in on. Now rotate the camera to the right until you find another exit. That is the route you will ascend after activating the Libra Gate Stone. Activate the stone and head to the next zone to find Gate Libra II. Unlock it and follow it up to the next zone. There are three more exits from this platform. Ascend the exit directly opposite from where you came in. This will take you to the Capricorn Gate Stone. Do not activate it yet. Instead, continue on to find a dead-end platform with a one-time guaranteed Ring of Renewal. Return to the Capricorn Gate Stone.

You have a long way to go from the Capricorn Gate Stone to the gate you need to open, so familiarize yourself with the instructions. In short, you are going to head back to Way Stone XVI and take the exit to the left from where you came in:

1. Activate the Capricorn Gate Stone
2. Head away from the dead end path by descending the other path
3. Head straight through the next section and descend the opposite path
4. Take a right at the next section to descend back to the Libra Gate Stone
5. Take a right at the Libra Gate Stone to descend to Way Stone XVI
6. Turn sharp left at Way Stone XVI to head down to Gate Capricorn I

Now you've reach the Virgo Gate Stone. You don't quite have as far to go with this one, but you still don't want to get lost.

1. Activate the Libra Gate Stone
2. Return back the way you came to Way Stone XVI
3. Turn sharp right at Way Stone XVI to reach the Libra Gate Stone
4. Turn left at the Libra Gate Stone to ascend to the platform with Gate Virgo I

Head up the path to the next platform and turn slightly left to reach Way Stone XVII. Use that to reach Way Stone XVIII. This is one of a few zones where the rare game Evil Spirit may appear in place of a Forbidden. The next zone has a Save Crystal.

There are three exits from the Save Crystal.

1. The right exit takes you to Way Stone XIX and the other platform where Evil Spirit may spawn. If you take Way Stone XIX, it will return you to Way Stone XX which now can be used to get back to this location quickly. If you aren't prepared to take on Omega Mark XII, it's a good idea to get that Way Stone activated now to save you time later.
2. The center exit takes you to a dead-end platform with Ultima.
3. The left exit takes you to the Aquarius Gate Stone.

If you want the goodies (Hastega magick and the Artemis Bow), descend the left exit. If you haven't spawned Larva Eater yet, you should go for the goodies. Larva Eater is a rare game that spawns after you've killed 256 enemies and has the ultimate dagger Mina as a rare drop. It spawned for me at Way Stone XVII after I picked up both Hastega and the Artemis Bow.

To get to the goodies, you need to do three things: Use Aquarius Gate Stone to access Taurus Gate Stone, change which Capricorn Gate is opened, and use the Taurus Gate Stone to access the goodies - all in that order. Each item is behind a different Taurus gate, so you will have to do the Taurus run twice. So, starting with accessing Taurus:

1. Activate the Aquarius Gate Stone
2. Run back to Way Stone XVIII and use it to return to Way Stone XVII
3. Head down the only exit and ascend the right path at the next platform
4. Go straight across the next platform and ascend the opposite path
5. Turn right at the next platform to descend to Gate Aquarius I
6. Open the Gate Aquarius I just in front of the next platform

You now have access to the path to the Taurus gate, but don't go there yet. Instead, go back up and take a sharp right to reach the Capricorn Gate. The Taurus gates are behind a Gate Capricorn II, so you need to open it first.

1. Activate the Capricorn Gate Stone
2. Return back using the path you came from
3. Head straight across the next platform and descend the opposite path
4. Turn right at the next platform to head down toward the Libra Gate Stone
5. Turn left at the Libra Gate Stone and descend to the next zone
6. Open Gate Capricorn II on the right side of the next platform

Now that the way is clear, head back down to the Libra Gate Stone and turn right. Turn left at the next platform with the Skulwyrm, and right at the following one with the Ash Wyrm to reach where you opened Gate Aquarius I. Continue following the path to the next platform to find the Taurus Gate Stone. The route from here to the two prizes only varies in the last step, so I will give them both here:

1. Activate the Taurus Gate Stone
2. Return back up the path you came from
3. Turn slightly right using the only available exit
4. Turn left at the next platform
5. Turn right at the next platform to descend to the Libra Gate Stone
6. Turn left at the Libra Gate Stone to reach where you unlocked Gate Capricorn II
7. Turn right through the only available exit (the last exit is blocked by Gate Virgo II)
8. Turn left to reach Gate Taurus II OR go straight across to reach Gate Taurus I
1. If you went left, try to open the gate while fighting the enemies, then take the other available exit to reach the dead-end platform with the Hastega magick
2. If you went across, try to open the gate while fighting enemies. The Artemis Bow will be on the platform. The other exit takes you to a series of gates leading to Omega Mark XII

To return to the Save Crystal near Ultima, make your way back to the Libra Gate Stone and take the only exit that ascends. Turn right at the next platform with the Skulwyrm and Forbidden, and left at the next to be back at Way Stone XVII. Use the way stone and head down to the Save Crystal. Be sure to save your game before taking the middle exit up to Ultima.

> ##### Esper: Ultima
>
>
> | **Level** | 63 | **LP** | 65 | **CP** | 6890 |
> | **HP** | 258001 | **Steal** | Virgo Gem (55/80%), Elixir (10/30%), Megalixir (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Dark |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Holy<br>\[Nullify\] Fire, Ice, Lightning, Water, Wind, Earth |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | Blind |
>
> This battle can be quite tough as Ultima will cause a magick field to occur that will cycle through different impairments. Starting with HP decrease, then MP decrease, no attack command, no magick command, no technick command, no item command, and longer weapon charge time. Additionally, Ultima will cast Reflectga on the party, which can be irritating when trying to keep your party buffed. Ultima will also use the high-powered Holyja spell (which you really don't want reflecting back). It can cause Reverse, however, and Ultima will attempt to cast Curaja or Renew on any character with Reverse on.
>
> There are a few things you can do to slow Ultima down. First, equip White Masks and Sage's Rings to as many characters as possible to absorb the Holy magick. Next, equip Dark-element weapons. Yagyu Darkblade with Genji Gear and Arcturus with Dark Shot are both helpful. If you don't have the Yagyu Darkblade, shame on you. Equip the Ashura as it causes Blindness on hit and Ultima will waste time casting Blindna if blinded. Have someone in the party that can cast Darkga (a Red Battlemage) when the attack command is sealed, and have that person wearing Black Robes. Since you won't be able to apply buffs, having Ring of Renewals and/or Bubble Belts equipped are a good idea - especially to deal with the HP drain field. Change your healing gambits to X-Potions or be ready to equip a Opal Ring on your healer if Reflectga gets cast. If you equip an Opal Ring, you can have one party member continue to buff while your Red Battlemage casts Darkga on your party once they all have Reflect status on. The trade off is that you might not be able to equip Holy-absorbing gear on the person wearing the Opal Ring if you don't have enough White Masks.
>
> When the MP reduction field is up, be sure to turn off your characters' Charge gambit so they don't waste time, and when the magick command is removed be sure to switch your gambits to attack. You have to be attentive and do some manual switching for this battle.
>
> Even with all the preparation, even a high-level party will likely run into difficulty if too many get afflicted with Reverse and you can't use Remedies fast enough. Just keep calm, toss out the Phoenix Downs, and get back on your feet. Absorbing Holy magick and dealing Dark damage will eventually turn the battle back in your favor.

#### Zodiark, Keeper of the Precepts

The quest for the toughest Esper begins in **Jahara / Lull of the Land**. Find Geomancer Yugelu in the northeast corner after you have 10 espers available on your License Board. He will unlock new areas in the Henne Mines, where you may find Zodiark. Teleport to **Henne Mines / Staging Shaft**.

| **Treasures** | 15 ( **Phase 2 Dig**), 8 ( **Crossover C**), 5 ( **Phase 2 Shaft**), 15 ( **Special Charter Shaft**), |
| **Notable Loot** | Henne Mines Candle, Dueling Mask, Fomalhaut, Nihopalaoa, Grand Helm, Circlet, Makara, Mythril Sword, Bubble Belt, Germinas Boots, Sage's Ring, Zodiac Spear, Renew (magick), Addle (technick), Brave Suit, Ribbon, Grand Armor, Lordly Robes |
| **Enemies** | Abysteel, Gizamaluk, Hecteyes, Glaring Eye (rare game), Necrofiend, Etém, Zodiark (esper) |

Head north into **Ore Separation** and take the now unlocked east exit to reach **Phase 2 Dig**. When the Minimap shows an option to go north, look south for an off-map spur that has a one-time guaranteed Dueling Mask at the end. Return to the east-west path and take it as far east as you can before turning south to find a one-time guaranteed Fomalhaut and the candle urn. Now take the path south that you just skipped. This path is fairly linear and takes you through some tiled path as well. When the tunnel turns northwest, start looking for an off-map passage to the northeast. There is a chance to find a one-time Nihopalaoa here. When there is a chance to go south or west, go west and follow the path to another dead-end. There may be a one-time Grand Helm here. There will also be a discarded paper hinting at some stash of treasure in the mines. Return to the previous intersection and continue south. This looks like a dead-end, but continues off-map. When you reach the off-map intersection, there is a one-time guaranteed Circlet on the east side among the crates. Continue south and look for a one-time guaranteed Makara in the brief on-map room. Head back north to the previous off-map intersection and go west. Look north for another off-map dead-end when you reach the west wall. There is a one-time guaranteed Mythril Sword here. Head south and follow the path around all the way to the next zone **Crossover C**.

In **Crossover C**, you will begin encountering Hecteyes. Their Mythril Bubbles can cause Confuse status, so have some Nu Khai Sand gambits ready. Be on the lookout for the rare game Glaring Eye whenever you defeat a Hecteye. This is a ghost like enemy. Thanks to the gates, this zone is fairly linear with a few random chest spawns. Just before the exit on the west side, look north for a one-time guaranteed Germinas Boots. A 25% one-time Bubble Belt may spawn on the trap at the intersection. If the Bubble Belt doesn't appear, since you are right next to an exit it is worth rezoning until it does.

Don't bother flipping the Gate Switchboard in **Pithead Junction C**, just continue to **Phase 2 Shaft**. It looks like flipping the switchboard would have made accessing **Feywood** possible but the path is collapsed. If you enter from **Feywood**, however, there is a chance for a one-time Sage's Ring. For now, wind your way to the east side of the map to reach **Special Charter Shaft**.

> ##### Esper: Zodiark
>
>
> | **Level** | 66 | **LP** | 70 | **CP** | 7610 |
> | **HP** | 336847 | **Steal** | Serpentarius (55/80%), Megalixir (10/30%), Great Trango (3/6%) |
> | **Elemental Weaknesses** | Holy |
> | **Elemental Resistances** | \[Absorb\] Dark |
> | **Status Susceptibilities** | N/A |
>
> There is a lot to think through when facing the ultimate esper. It starts with several buffs, but if you dispel them it makes the latter part of the battle tougher. If you don't, you have to deal with Zodiark's Reflect status and may need a caster with an Opal Ring. Halfway through the battle, Zodiark's elemental weakness will switch and you will want to switch to non-elemental weapons and non-elemental magick like Scathe, Scourge, and Shock. Zodiark likes to use Darkja, which can instantly KO anyone it hits. It's best to have two of your three characters wearing Black Masks to absorb the damage, but if all three have Black Masks equipped Zodiark will cast this twice as often - meaning twice the KOs. Zodiark has several level-dependent spells, so it's best not to have a level divisible by 2, 3, or 4 (71, 73, 77, and 79 are all good levels).
>
> Before the battle, equip your back-up characters with Pheasant Netsuke so they can restore more HP if they have to get swapped in to resurrect a fallen party. Also keep a couple of them with Dark-absorbing gear like Black Masks or a Demon Shield. If you have to use them, be sure that the back-up party is in decent health when they get swapped back out so that they won't be wiped out if needed again.
>
> Begin the battle with only a single person in your party, preferably one of your back-up characters. Zodiark will immediately cast Darkja, so you don't want your whole primary party exposed to a potential KO right off the bat. Then swap in the rest. If you can equip holy-based weapons to start off with (like Excalibur), do so. You will need to swap it out later when Zodiark starts changing weaknesses. Be sure your magick-users are ready to use Hi-Ethers instead of Charge to reduce the number of turns they are down.
>
> Even with all this, the battle gets incredibly difficult once Zodiark drops below 20% HP. Do your best to swap out your Scourge caster whenever Darkja is about to be cast, and make casting Scourge their top priority. This is the best way to survive. Keep using Phoenix Downs on your fallen party members that have been swapped to Reserve. If you can, use up everyone's Quickenings before the physical immunity starts.
